## L. SourceLocation Contract Closure — 2026-08-31

Per the post-D7 directive's Phase A. Closes the one AMBIGUITY §J flagged (SCIP Item "Range boundary convention").

**Inspection performed before coding:** grepped HLRD.md and TAD.md for `SourceLocation` (zero matches — the type name itself appears nowhere) and for "source location"/"line number"/"column" (TAD §12 lists it as a `RepositorySymbol` field and TAD §71's traceability diagram names it as the last link in the evidence chain; HLRD only lists "source locations" as one of several things SCIP *may* provide). **Neither document ever states a numbering or boundary convention** — confirmed not a contradiction (nothing to reconcile), a genuine silent gap. Inspected every current populator: `SCIPAdapter._location_from_range` (the only code path that sets `RepositorySymbol.source_location`, confirmed by `grep -r source_location src/`); `GitAdapter` (no location data at all — `HISTORY` is a whole-file lifecycle fact); `CodeQLAdapter` (uses SARIF `region.startLine` internally, for codeFlow endpoint matching only — never attaches a `SourceLocation` to any entity, confirmed by inspection of `_resolve_location`/`_flow_endpoints`). Re-fetched the authoritative OASIS SARIF 2.1.0 schema's `region` definition directly to confirm its convention rather than assume it: `startLine`/`startColumn`/`endLine`/`endColumn` are all **1-based** (`"minimum": 1`), `endLine` is documented as *inclusive* ("the line number of the last character in the region"), and `endColumn` is documented as *exclusive* ("the column number of the character following the end of the region") — structurally the same half-open-at-the-end model SCIP already uses, offset by exactly 1 on all four numbers.

**Resolved as a specification clarification, not an ADR** (directive Phase A §6): no competing designs or tradeoffs exist to decide between — SCIP (the only current populator) already emits one specific, spec-derived convention, and adopting it as the canonical one requires no behavior change to any existing adapter. TAD §12 amended in place with an inline clarifying note (see TAD's own amendment log); no HLRD change was needed (HLRD never attempted to define a convention).

**Canonical convention:** 0-based, half-open (`[start, end)`), matching SCIP/LSP/tree-sitter. `start_column`/`end_column` optional together, never independently (a location may be line-only). No in-type "whole file"/"unknown" sentinel — `RepositorySymbol.source_location = None` already serves that purpose (used today for `EXTERNAL_LIBRARY` entities). `file_path` is repository-root-relative, `/`-separated, matching `build_canonical_id`'s `qualified_name` convention. `repository_id`/`revision` deliberately excluded from the type itself (always inherited from the containing `RepositorySymbol` — adding them would invent redundant, driftable state).

**Code change:** `codex.ontology.entities.SourceLocation` gained field-level bounds (`start_line`/`end_line`/`start_column`/`end_column` all `ge=0`, via pydantic `Field`) and a `model_validator` enforcing `end_line >= start_line`, both columns present-or-absent together, and `end_column >= start_column` when `start_line == end_line`. This tightens a previously fully-unvalidated type into a deterministic contract without changing any existing adapter's behavior — confirmed by all 275 pre-existing tests (including all SCIP tests, whose real-fixture-derived `SourceLocation` values already satisfy the new constraints) passing unmodified. No provider adapter code was changed — SCIP was already conformant by construction; CodeQL and Git don't currently populate this field at all, so there was nothing to convert yet, only a documented conversion rule for when a future capability does.

**Tests:** 12 new tests in `tests/test_source_location.py` — line-only validity, full-precision validity, zero-width (empty) range validity, multi-line ranges (no cross-line column-ordering constraint), rejection of `end_line < start_line`, rejection of `end_column < start_column` on the same line, rejection of partial column precision, rejection of negative coordinates (parametrized across all four fields), and deterministic structural equality for identical inputs. 287 tests total project-wide, 100% coverage on `codex/ontology/entities.py`, clean ruff/mypy.

**Cross-document effects checked (directive Phase A §6):** Entity Resolution (not yet built — will consume `SourceLocation` only as a tie-breaking/corroboration signal per Phase B below, never as primary identity); Evidence (does **not** carry a `SourceLocation` field at all — confirmed by inspection of `codex.evidence.model.Evidence`, which has `raw_reference` but no location field, so this closure has no direct effect there); `raw_reference` (a separate, already-closed URI-scheme contract, unaffected); source-context retrieval/Verification (both `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, no effect yet); historical graph queries/`graph_version` (unaffected — `SourceLocation` carries no version-dependent state beyond what its containing `RepositorySymbol` already versions). No genuine architectural decision requiring a STOP was found.

## M. Entity Resolution — 2026-08-31

Per the post-D7 directive's Phase B. TAD component #7 ("Entity Resolution + Reconciliation Engine") is split into two directive phases; this section covers Entity Resolution only (§N covers Reconciliation).

**Pre-audit findings (directive Phase B §7), from direct inspection of `build_canonical_id`, `RepositorySymbol`, and all three provider adapters — not assumed from prior reports:**

- `FILE` is the **only** base type every current provider can produce: `GitAdapter`, `SCIPAdapter` (file references), and `CodeQLAdapter` all call `build_canonical_id(..., base_type=BaseEntityType.FILE, ...)`. Non-FILE (CLASS/METHOD/...) entities come from `SCIPAdapter` alone — "cross-provider symbol convergence" (directive §12) has no live case with the current three providers; recorded honestly as such rather than exercised with a fabricated second symbol-producing provider.
- `build_canonical_id` already gives *exact* convergence for byte-identical inputs (proven by existing D3/D5/D6 tests). The real, previously undemonstrated risk: each adapter derives its FILE `qualified_name` from its own provider format (`git`'s diff output; SCIP's `Document.relative_path`; SARIF's `artifactLocation.uri`, typed as an open `uri-reference` by the OASIS schema — nothing requires it to already be a bare, repo-relative string). Real fixtures inspected in this and prior phases are all already-canonical plain relative paths (no live defect observed), but nothing previously enforced this.
- `RepositorySymbol.provider_ids` (TAD's own field) is populated only by `GitAdapter` (`{"git": path}`) today; SCIP/CodeQL never set it — sparse, not a defect, but merge logic must not drop it.
- **A real, previously undiscovered defect found by this audit**: `IngestionPipeline._commit_entities` (D4) kept only the *last-committed* `RepositorySymbol` per `canonical_id` on a collision — silently discarding an earlier provider's `roles`/`provider_ids` contribution, directly contradicting D4's own documented "no evidence is ever discarded" invariant (`codex/ingestion/pipeline.py`'s own module docstring). Never triggered by any existing test (no two providers previously produced a colliding `canonical_id` in any test scenario), so it shipped unnoticed through D4-D6's own review passes. Fixed as part of this phase (see below) — this is the "concrete conformance defect" that justifies touching D4's `_commit_entities`/`_commit_relationships`, per the directive's own "unless an actual conformance defect is discovered" carve-out (directive top matter, and §25).

**Design (directive Phase B §8-11):** `codex.resolution.entity_resolver.resolve_entities()` — deterministic, no LLM/SLM/embeddings/fuzzy matching, no second identity system. Two match classes only:

1. **`EXACT_CANONICAL_ID`** — two provider entities already share a `canonical_id` (byte-identical inputs). → MERGE.
2. **`NORMALIZED_PATH_IDENTITY`** — a FILE/DIRECTORY entity's `qualified_name`, run through a new `codex.resolution.paths.normalize_repo_relative_path()` (strips `\`→`/`, leading `./`, leading `/`, collapses `//`), recomputes to a *different* `canonical_id` than another entity already holds. → MERGE, with the **recomputed, normalized** id as the definitive canonical id (never whichever raw entity happened to sort first — order-independence proven by a dedicated test). Non-path-shaped entities (SCIP symbols, `EXTERNAL_LIBRARY` package identities) are never renormalized — their own provider-computed id is trusted as-is, per "only use signals the architecture already supports."

No **ambiguous-match** or **conflicting-identity** case exists in the current three-provider set: `canonical_id` already includes `base_type`/`qualified_name`/`revision` in its hash, so two genuinely different entities (different name, different namespace, different revision, different base type) can never collide, and two entities that DO collide are, by construction, the same real entity — there is no case where the current deterministic signals produce a genuine ambiguity. This is recorded honestly as "structurally prevented, not yet exercised," not assumed to generalize to future providers without re-auditing.

**Merge semantics (directive Phase B §10, provenance-preserving):** `roles` — union, order-preserving, deduplicated. `provider_ids` — dict union (a real key collision with different values would be a genuine conflicting-identity signal; none currently possible since each provider uses its own provider-name key). `lifecycle_status` — a non-default (DELETED/RENAMED) value from either side wins over a plain default ACTIVE from the other, since only `GitAdapter` ever observes lifecycle and ACTIVE-by-default carries no information; proven order-independent (informative side may be either the first- or second-processed entity). `source_location` — first non-`None` wins (deterministic tie-break for a case no current provider pair actually produces two different locations for one entity — documented, not exercised by real data).

**`IngestionPipeline` integration:** `_commit_entities` now re-resolves the full accumulated entity set (previous + this batch) through `resolve_entities()` on every commit — O(N) per call (not O(N²): `resolve_entities` itself is a single dict-keyed pass), an accepted V1-scale cost per the directive's own "do not prematurely optimize without measurements" (§28). Returns a raw-id → resolved-id map; `_commit_evidence` now remaps `Evidence.subject`/`.object` through this map **before** keying `CanonicalRelationship`s (the stored `Evidence` record's own `subject`/`object` fields are never mutated — only the relationship key uses resolved ids), so a normalized-path merge that renames a raw provider id never leaves a relationship dangling on the old one. Proven end-to-end by `test_entity_resolution_converges_differently_formatted_paths_through_the_pipeline` (`tests/test_ingestion_pipeline.py`): two fake providers report the same real file with a canonical and a `./`-prefixed path; the pipeline resolves to one FILE entity, and the second provider's relationship evidence attaches to the *canonical* id, not the raw one.

**Graph version behavior (directive Phase B §13):** unaffected — `resolve_entities()` is a pure function of static entity fields (never mutates a previously-published `GraphVersion`); `IngestionPipeline._materialize_store()` still builds a fresh, immutable store/version per `run()` from the accumulator's state at that moment, exactly as D4 already established. No change to that invariant was needed.

**Tests:** 23 new tests in `tests/test_entity_resolution.py` (path normalization ×5, exact/cross-provider FILE convergence ×2, normalized-path convergence + order-independence ×3, non-merge cases — different directory/base-type/revision ×3, symbol-entity non-renormalization honesty ×1, external-library convergence + version-significance ×2, provenance preservation — provider_ids/roles/lifecycle ×4, source_location tie-break ×1, empty/single-entity edge cases ×2), plus 1 new `IngestionPipeline` integration test. 311 tests total project-wide, 100% coverage on `codex/resolution/`, clean ruff/mypy. `CanonicalRelationship` gained a `contradiction_score: float` field (default `0.0`, backward compatible) in `codex/evidence/model.py`, added now because Reconciliation (§N) needs somewhere to store TAD §38's formula output — not otherwise used by this phase.

No new ADR — this closes TAD component #7's Entity Resolution half using signals the architecture (canonical id hashing, `provider_ids`) already establishes; no genuinely new architectural decision was required. No cross-document contradiction found. D1/D2/D3/D5/D6 untouched; D4's `_commit_entities`/`_commit_relationships` were changed, justified by the concrete defect found above, not as an unrelated contract change.

## N. Evidence Reconciliation — 2026-08-31

Per the post-D7 directive's Phase C. Closes TAD component #7's second half.

**A genuine finding, flagged rather than silently resolved (directive Rule 0.2):** the directive's own §15 ("Reconciliation Status Taxonomy") states the canonical taxonomy is "`VERIFIED/PARTIALLY_VERIFIED/QUALIFIED/DISPUTED/INCONCLUSIVE/REJECTED`." Direct inspection of `docs/TAD.md` shows this is **not** the enum that governs `CanonicalRelationship.status` — that field's type is `EvidenceStatus` (TAD §18, `SUPPORTED/WEAKLY_SUPPORTED/DISPUTED/UNRESOLVED/CONTRADICTED/UNSUPPORTED`), already implemented since Phase 1 and closed. The six-value list the directive names is TAD §50's, a **different, later-stage** taxonomy for the Verification Engine's answer/claim-level output (component #9/DTD-05, downstream of LLM synthesis) — confirmed by reading both enum definitions directly (TAD line ~539 vs. line ~1338), not assumed. TAD is internally consistent here (two distinct, correctly section-scoped enums, with an existing C-3-reconciled mapping between them); the conflation is only in the directive's own paraphrase, not a genuine cross-document contradiction, so this did **not** trigger a STOP (directive §32 condition #5/#7 requires an actual contradiction between authoritative documents, and none exists). Resolved by using `EvidenceStatus` — TAD §18's own, purpose-built field — for this phase, and leaving TAD §50's taxonomy untouched and out of scope (correctly deferred to the not-yet-built Verification Engine, per the directive's own §23 instruction not to implement it prematurely).

**Pre-audit findings (directive Phase C §14):**

- `CanonicalRelationship.supporting_evidence_ids`/`.contradicting_evidence_ids` already exist (TAD §73) — populated, not invented.
- `Evidence.effective_independence_group` (TAD §16) already correctly implements "omitted group defaults to non-independent *within the same provider*" (`provider_default:{provider}` — different providers get different default groups, i.e. independent by default, matching TAD §16's own two-part text: `independence_group = provider_default_family`, "if omitted, SHALL be treated as non-independent"). Reused as-is.
- **`provider_authority` (TAD §38's own formula input) has no defined source anywhere in HLRD/TAD** — the same class of gap ADR-018 already resolved for `evidence_quality`/`cost_factor` (TAD §31). Resolved the same way (explicit, `[0,1]`-normalized, caller-supplied) but **not** added to `codex.registry.scoring.ProviderScoreProfile` — that type is scoped to TAD §31's formula and closed (D2/ADR-018); extending it would force every existing D2/D3/D5/D6 test that constructs one to pass a new field it has no reason to care about. Instead, `IngestionPipeline` gained its own `provider_authority: Mapping[str, float] | None` constructor parameter, defaulting missing entries to full trust (`1.0`) rather than raising — deliberately different from ADR-018's "no default" choice, because this factor weights *confidence* on an already-committed relationship rather than *selecting* a provider, so silently under-trusting an unconfigured provider would be a worse default than full trust, and this choice keeps every pre-existing test passing unmodified.
- **No negation mechanism exists anywhere in the ontology.** `RelationshipType` has no "does-not-X" counterpart, and `Evidence` has no boolean/flag for asserting a negative fact — confirmed by inspecting all three real adapters' `RelationshipType` usage (D5/D6 closure audits, §J). The directive's own DISPUTED example ("SCIP → CALLS, Provider-X → DOES_NOT_CALL") is therefore **not representable** by any current provider. This is an intentional, documented limitation, not a silent gap: the algorithm implements the full TAD §38 formula and all six `EvidenceStatus` values generically (proven with handcrafted evidence in `tests/test_reconciler.py`), but `IngestionPipeline._reconcile_relationships` always calls it with `contradicting=()` in production, since there is no deterministic signal available to populate it without inventing one — `DISPUTED`/`CONTRADICTED`/`UNSUPPORTED` are correct-by-construction but not reachable end-to-end with Git/SCIP/CodeQL today.

**Algorithm (`codex.reconciliation.reconciler.reconcile_relationship`):** a relationship whose subject or object isn't a currently-resolved entity is `UNRESOLVED` (checked first — directive §19-20's "missing target is an information/system gap, not a dispute"). Otherwise: `weight = evidence.confidence × provider_authority × freshness_decay` (the freshness term reuses D2/ADR-018's already-established `default_freshness_score`, directly addressing directive §17/§22's "stale evidence affects confidence" — not reinvented). `contradiction_score = Σcontradict_weight / (Σsupport_weight + Σcontradict_weight)`, exactly TAD §38's literal formula (verified against the arithmetic in a dedicated test, not just direction). Confidence combines *supporting* evidence with an independence-aware rule: same-`effective_independence_group` records collapse to their single highest weight first (no double-counting correlated evidence — directive §16/§22), independent groups combine via `1 - Π(1 - weight)` (a standard, deterministic probability-combination rule — not a learned/probabilistic *identity* mechanism, which is what the "no probabilistic matching" prohibition in directive §8 targets for Entity Resolution specifically). Status: both supporting and contradicting present → `DISPUTED` (or `CONTRADICTED` if contradiction dominates, `contradiction_score >= 0.5`, a documented calibration point, not derived from HLRD/TAD, mirroring ADR-018's own framing of its freshness half-life); contradicting-only → `UNSUPPORTED`; supporting-only → `SUPPORTED` (support weight `>= 0.5`, same calibration-point status) or `WEAKLY_SUPPORTED`; neither → `UNRESOLVED`.

**`IngestionPipeline` integration:** a new `_reconcile_relationships()` step runs once per `run()`, after all providers commit and before `_publish()`, recomputing every accumulated relationship's `status`/`confidence`/`contradiction_score` from its full `Evidence` records (fetched from the `EvidenceStore` by id) against the current resolved entity set — not incrementally per evidence commit, so a relationship's status always reflects everything known about it at publish time, consistent with D4's "one new, immutable, published `GraphVersion` per `run()`" model. Two pre-existing tests' assertions were updated to match the now-real behavior (`tests/test_ingestion_pipeline.py::test_multiple_providers_contradictory_evidence_all_preserved`'s stale comment "No Reconciliation Engine exists yet" replaced with the actual reason its relationship stays `UNRESOLVED` — its fake providers never report entities, so the relationship's endpoints are genuinely unknown, not merely unreconciled; `tests/test_codeql_adapter.py::test_contradictory_evidence_between_git_and_codeql_both_preserved`'s obsolete `UNRESOLVED` assertion replaced with `SUPPORTED`/`WEAKLY_SUPPORTED`, matching what Reconciliation now correctly computes from real Git+CodeQL evidence).

**Tests:** 16 new tests in `tests/test_reconciler.py` (missing-target → `UNRESOLVED` ×3, single-provider support ×2, independent-vs-correlated confidence combination ×4, support+contradiction → `DISPUTED`/`CONTRADICTED`/`UNSUPPORTED` ×3, exact TAD §38 formula verification ×1, provider-failure-never-contradicts ×1, staleness reduces confidence ×1, provenance preservation ×1), plus 2 new `IngestionPipeline` integration tests (real `SUPPORTED` status through the pipeline; `provider_authority` constructor parameter proven to reach the Reconciler). 329 tests total project-wide, 100% coverage on `codex/reconciliation/` and `codex/ingestion/pipeline.py`, clean ruff/mypy.

No new ADR — `provider_authority`'s sourcing gap is resolved the same way ADR-018 already established the pattern for, not a new architectural decision. No cross-document contradiction found in the authoritative documents themselves (only in the directive's own paraphrase, addressed above). D1/D2/D3/D5/D6 untouched; D4's `run()` gained one new step (`_reconcile_relationships`) and its constructor gained one new optional parameter — additive, not a breaking contract change, confirmed by all pre-existing tests passing with at most a comment/assertion update reflecting genuinely new (and correct) behavior, never a workaround.

## O. Remaining Gap Inventory + Provider Overlap Re-Audit — 2026-08-31

Per the gap-closure directive's Phases 1-3. Phase 1's dependency reconstruction confirmed a clean, forward-only import graph (`ontology`/`repository` → `evidence` → `provider` → `registry` → `resolution`/`reconciliation` → `ingestion` → `graph`, verified by direct grep of every cross-module import, not assumed) and found **zero** reverse dependencies (`Planner→LLM`, `Provider→Planner`, `Graph→LLM`, `Reconciliation→LLM`, `Verification→Provider`) — none of Query Understanding/Planning/Verification/LLM exist yet, so there is nothing to check beyond confirming no current module imports anything LLM/SLM-shaped (confirmed: zero non-docstring hits).

| Gap | Source | Severity | Blocks | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** — SCIP `"."` manager/version placeholder | D5 closure audit §J (DEFERRED) | Low (identity-string cosmetics, not a graph-correctness defect) | Nothing directly; left unfixed it would misrepresent an unset field as a literal manager named `"."` | **CLOSED.** Confirmed against the reference `scip` Rust crate's `bindings/rust/src/symbol.rs` (fetched directly, read only for documented format/parse behavior): `"."` means "field unset," round-trips to `""` in the reference implementation's own test suite. Fixed in `codex.provider.scip.mapping.parse_symbol()` (normalizes `"."` → `""`); 5 new tests; `docs/resources.md`'s "Fourth material finding." |
| **B** — Coverage/Completeness Engine | HLRD/TAD; new directive Gap B | High (negative-query safety is an explicit correctness boundary) | DTD-02+ (Query Understanding/Planning/Verification) would otherwise have no deterministic way to distinguish "nothing found" from "couldn't fully search" | **PARTIALLY CLOSED.** TAD §34's negative-query rule and the six capability/provider distinctions are fully specified and now implemented (`codex.coverage.engine`). TAD §33's `LOW ≥50%/MEDIUM ≥75%/HIGH ≥90%` thresholds are **not** implementable — TAD never defines the coverage *metric* (percentage of what?). Not invented; `CompletenessLevel` enum defined for vocabulary, only `EXHAUSTIVE` has a working check. **OPEN, non-blocking**: the LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH metric definition is a genuine missing formula requiring a human decision, not a STOP on the whole directive (nothing currently depends on graded completeness — only exhaustive/negative-query safety, which is fully implemented). |
| **C** — EvidenceCohort completeness | New directive Gap C | Low (verification only) | Nothing — already correct | **CLOSED.** All required fields already present and correctly populated since D1-D6 (verified by direct inspection, not re-trusted). The four distinctions (not supported/unavailable/failed/empty-success) are now also the Coverage Engine's own test matrix (`tests/test_coverage_engine.py`), which doubles as Gap C's verification. |
| **D** — Graph version consistency | New directive Gap D | Low (mostly N/A — no downstream consumer yet) | Nothing currently — flagged for when Retrieval/Verification exist | **CLOSED for what exists; correctly N/A for what doesn't.** `GraphVersion` immutability-after-publish already tested (D4, `test_graph_version_is_published_and_immutable`); concurrent-update handling is structural (never mutate a returned version) per D4's own documented Phase-1 simplification, not lock-based — unchanged, no defect found. Query-level caching, historical reconstruction consumption, and "verification consumes the same version as retrieval" are all genuinely inapplicable — those components (`NOT_IMPLEMENTED`) don't exist to audit yet; recorded honestly as N/A rather than fabricating a check against code that isn't there. |
| **E** — Evidence independence | New directive Gap E | Low (verification only) | Nothing — already correct | **CLOSED.** `Evidence.effective_independence_group`'s "omitted group defaults to non-independent *within the same provider*" behavior re-confirmed correct against TAD §16's own two-part text (§N, previous phase) and covered by `tests/test_reconciler.py`'s independence tests. No drift found, no new tests needed. |
| **F** — Reconciliation semantics | New directive Gap F | Low (one deliberately-deferred refinement, not a defect) | Nothing blocking — documented, not silently skipped | **CLOSED, with one documented, deliberate non-implementation.** Support weighting, independence-aware combination, contradiction detection/score, freshness, provider authority, confidence, and evidence status are all implemented and tested (§N). "Coverage" (this gap's own word) is **not** folded into the confidence formula — `EvidenceCohort.coverage_status` is tracked but Reconciliation does not discount confidence for `PARTIAL`-cohort evidence, since TAD defines no such discount formula; inventing one would violate Rule 0.2. Explicitly recorded as a deferred refinement, not silently missing. |
| **G** — SourceLocation provider→graph path | New directive Gap G | Low (verification gap, not a code defect) | Nothing — real data already conforms | **CLOSED.** Previously only verified via a handcrafted fixture (`_linked_index()`) at the adapter level. Added `test_real_artifact_source_location_flows_through_to_the_graph` (`tests/test_scip_adapter.py`): decodes the real `typescript_sample.scip` fixture, runs it through the real `IngestionPipeline`, and confirms a real `Greeter` class definition's `SourceLocation` reaches the `GraphStore` with the correct `file_path` convention and passes the model's own validator (would raise `ValidationError` on a malformed real-world range — it doesn't). Git/CodeQL confirmed to still not populate `SourceLocation` at all (unchanged, correctly out of scope). |

### Phase 3 — Provider overlap re-audit

No new provider was added or proposed since the D7 STOP; the capability matrix is unchanged from `docs/architecture-conformance-audit.md`'s §K:

| Capability | Git | SCIP | CodeQL | D7 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FILE identity | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | deferred |
| Symbol definition/reference | | ✓ | | deferred |

## P. D8 — Query Understanding Engine: Traceability Matrix + Pre-Implementation Audit — 2026-08-31

Per the D8 directive's Phases 0-3. Re-read HLRD §24-31 and TAD §22-29 in full directly (not from prior conversation summaries) before writing any code.

### P.1 Traceability matrix (directive Phase 1)

Capture graph_version. 2. Store it in RetrievalPlan") | Query Understanding never touches the graph (directive Phase 4's explicit constraint) — no graph_version exists for it to capture | None | Traced; **not** added to `QueryContract` (would invent a field neither TAD's struct nor the graph-version-lock instructions assign to this stage) |

**Granularity note (intent vocabulary):** HLRD §28 gives a broader classification (`CODE_LOOKUP`, `CALL_GRAPH`, ...) while HLRD §30 and TAD §23's own worked Tier-0 example use a more specific, operation-shaped vocabulary (`FIND_CALLERS`, ...). These are not in conflict — nothing in either document asserts one vocabulary excludes the other — but they describe intent at two different levels of granularity without an explicit mapping between them. Resolved as a **documented interpretation** (directive Phase 19, category 2 — not a STOP-worthy contradiction): `QueryContract.intent` uses the specific, operation-shaped vocabulary, because that is what TAD §23 concretely demonstrates flowing from Tier-0 into a contract, and because HLRD §30 explicitly frames it as what "the planner shall convert user intent into" — i.e., HLRD's own two lists already describe a category→operation relationship, and V1 skips the intermediate coarse category by detecting the specific operation directly wherever a deterministic pattern exists. HLRD §28's broader categories are not implemented as a separate field or enum in V1.

### P.2 Gap/contradiction audit (directive Phase 2-3)

Every specific clarification the directive restates (complexity weights, Tier-0/SLM thresholds, session context window, provider scoring weights, budget-pruning steps, negative-query rule) was checked directly against TAD's text, not assumed correct because a prior directive said so:

- **Complexity weights** (TAD §26): `intent_count 0.25, target_count 0.15, relationship_depth 0.25, ambiguity 0.15, temporal_dimension 0.10, reasoning_requirement 0.10` — **confirmed exact match**, sums to 1.0.
- **Tier-0 routing** (TAD §23): `>0.95` deterministic, `0.70-0.95` SLM disambiguation, `<0.70` SLM — **confirmed exact match**.
- **SLM confidence policy** (TAD §25): `>0.85` execute, `0.50-0.85` execute+clarify, `<0.50` escalate to LLM; "calibrated probability... not raw logits" — **confirmed exact match**.
- **Session context** (TAD §28): one repository; last 10 queries OR 30 minutes, whichever first; repo change resets; older context lower-weighted — **confirmed exact match**.
- **Provider scoring weights** (TAD §31): `capability_match 0.40, evidence_quality 0.20, availability 0.15, freshness 0.15, cost_factor 0.10` — **confirmed exact match against the already-implemented, closed `codex.registry.scoring.PROVIDER_SCORE_WEIGHTS`**; this is D2 territory, not touched or reopened by D8.
- **Budget pruning** (TAD §41-42, referenced by the directive): matches the already-established Planner-phase rules; **not implemented in D8** (D8 produces a `QueryContract` with `token_budget`/`latency_budget` fields populated from the query, but does not itself run pruning — that is explicitly the Query Planner's job, TAD §29, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and correctly out of D8's scope).
- **Negative-query rule** (TAD §34): unchanged, already implemented in `codex.coverage` (previous phase); D8 only needs to set `completeness_requirement = EXHAUSTIVE` for negative-shaped queries, not evaluate coverage itself.
- **TAD §33 completeness percentage denominator** (the one previously-flagged open gap): re-confirmed still undefined in the current `docs/TAD.md` (re-read directly, no change since the prior gap-closure pass). **Determined not to block D8**: Query Understanding only needs to select which `CompletenessLevel` a query requires (an enum classification, driven by query phrasing — e.g. a negative/exhaustive-shaped query selects `EXHAUSTIVE`), never to compute or verify an achieved percentage against a metric. Recorded per the directive's own instruction: **"OPEN — DEFERRED TO PLANNER/COVERAGE CALIBRATION."** Not invented, not silently resolved, does not block D8.

No genuine HLRD↔TAD contradiction, no QueryContract field defined differently across documents, no conflict with an already-approved D1-D6/Entity-Resolution/Reconciliation/Coverage contract, and no requirement forcing D7 to be reopened. **No STOP condition triggered.** Proceeding to implementation.

## Q. D8 — Query Understanding Engine: Cross-DTD Audit After Implementation — 2026-08-31

Package: `src/codex/query_understanding/` (`models.py`, `complexity.py`, `tier0.py`, `slm.py`, `session.py`, `engine.py`, `__init__.py`). Tests: `tests/test_qu_tier0.py`, `tests/test_qu_complexity.py`, `tests/test_qu_session.py`, `tests/test_qu_contract.py`, `tests/test_qu_engine.py`, `tests/test_qu_security.py`, `tests/test_qu_boundaries.py`, `tests/fake_slm_adapter.py`. 78 new tests, 100% coverage on all seven new modules. Validated in a fresh venv: `ruff check src tests` clean, `mypy src` clean (45 source files), full suite 435 passed.

### A. Requirements implemented in full

All ~22 items traced in P.1 as "Traced" are implemented: `QueryContract` (exact TAD §27 field list, no invented fields), the deterministic Tier-0 detector (`tier0.detect`, TAD §23 thresholds), the `SLMAdapter` Protocol boundary (TAD §24-25, interface only), session context (TAD §28, one-repository/10-query/30-minute/decay/repo-change-reset), the exact TAD §26 complexity formula and weights, `UnderstandingStatus` (RESOLVED/SLM_UNAVAILABLE/LLM_ESCALATION_REQUIRED) honestly representing the three real outcomes rather than fabricating a contract when no SLM is configured or confidence is below the escalate floor, `required_evidence` derivation per intent, and the security/validation requirements (query text treated strictly as data; malformed contracts rejected by pydantic validators).

### B. Requirements partially implemented

None. Every item traced as "Traced" in P.1 has a corresponding implementation and test; items explicitly out of scope (cache, budget pruning, negative-query coverage evaluation, graph-version capture) are full non-implementations, not partial ones — see C.

### C. Requirements intentionally deferred (not blocking D8)

- **Query Understanding Cache** (TAD §46) — no cache layer exists anywhere in Codex yet; would be scope creep ahead of the components that actually need one.
- **Budget pruning** (TAD §41-42) — Query Planner's responsibility (`NOT_IMPLEMENTED`), not Query Understanding's; D8 only populates `token_budget`/`latency_budget` from defaults.
- **Negative-query coverage evaluation** (TAD §34) — already implemented in `codex.coverage` from a prior phase; D8's only obligation is classifying `completeness_requirement`, which it does.
- **Graph-version capture** — explicitly the Planner's job per the original "GRAPH VERSION LOCK" instructions; Query Understanding never touches the graph, so it has no version to capture. `QueryContract` deliberately has no `graph_version` field (tested structurally in `test_qu_boundaries.py`).
- **TAD §33 completeness-percentage metric** — genuinely undefined in TAD itself (not a Codex gap); does not block D8 since D8 only selects a `CompletenessLevel`, never computes an achieved percentage.

### D. New ambiguities discovered during implementation

None beyond the one already resolved in P.1 (intent-vocabulary granularity, HLRD §28 vs. §30/TAD §23) and the one already flagged in P.2 (TAD §33 metric). Implementation did not surface any new HLRD/TAD gap not already anticipated pre-implementation.

### E. Cross-document contradictions found

None. HLRD and TAD remain mutually consistent on every point checked (P.2); the intent-vocabulary difference is a granularity difference, not a contradiction (P.1 Granularity note).

### F. Silent implementation choices avoided / documented interpretations made

Per directive Phase 19's classification scheme, all non-trivial choices were classified explicitly rather than made silently:
- **Implementation detail** (category 1): `TemporalDimension` enum values (`NONE/POINT_IN_TIME/RANGE/HISTORICAL`) — TAD names the field but not its values; `_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE` intent→Capability mapping table — TAD does not enumerate this mapping, and no downstream component depends on its exact contents yet; `DECAY_HALF_LIFE_QUERIES = 5.0` — a documented calibration point, same pattern as ADR-018's freshness half-life.
- **Documented interpretation** (category 2): intent-vocabulary granularity (P.1); `constraints[]` typed as `list[str]` (no schema given anywhere).
- No category-3 (ADR-required) or category-4 (STOP) decisions were needed for D8.

### G. Security concerns identified and how addressed

Query text is user-controlled and must never be interpreted as instructions. Addressed by construction: Tier-0 only ever produces a bounded enum (`Intent`) plus regex-captured substrings used solely as opaque `targets` strings — it cannot set `token_budget`, `latency_budget`, or `completeness_requirement` from text content (all three are fixed defaults or SLM-derived, never text-derived, and are covered by dedicated injection tests in `test_qu_security.py`). The SLM boundary is a typed Protocol returning a validated `SLMInterpretation` pydantic model — even a compromised/malicious SLM output is bounded by pydantic's field types and value ranges, and query text that impersonates a "system directive" but matches no Tier-0 pattern is inert, proven structurally by `test_injection_attempt_is_treated_as_ordinary_unmatched_text`. `QueryContract`'s own validators reject out-of-range confidence/complexity/ambiguity and non-positive budgets regardless of source.

### H. Dependency-boundary violations found

None. `test_qu_boundaries.py` programmatically parses every module's imports via `ast` and asserts none of `codex.provider.contract`, `codex.provider.git_adapter`, `codex.provider.scip_adapter`, `codex.provider.codeql_adapter`, `codex.provider.scip`, `codex.registry`, `codex.graph`, `codex.ingestion`, `codex.resolution`, or `codex.reconciliation` are imported anywhere in the package. The one deliberate exception, `codex.provider.capability.Capability`, is a stateless vocabulary enum with no provider-selection or extraction behavior, reused the same way `RelationshipType` and `CompletenessLevel` already are — documented inline in the boundary test itself.

### I. Tests proving each architectural invariant

- **No graph access**: `test_no_graph_access_result_never_carries_graph_data`, `test_query_contract_has_no_graph_or_provider_fields`.
- **No provider/registry/graph/ingestion/resolution/reconciliation imports**: `test_no_module_imports_graph_provider_registry_ingestion_resolution_reconciliation` (AST-based, structural, not convention-based).
- **No real model dependency**: `test_no_llm_or_slm_model_dependency_imported`.
- **No LLM answer generation**: `test_no_llm_answer_generation_llm_escalation_never_carries_an_answer`.
- **SLM not invoked for every query**: `test_slm_not_invoked_for_deterministic_query`.
- **Calibrated-probability semantics**: `test_slm_confidence_is_bounded_to_calibrated_probability_range`, confidence-band routing tests.
- **Determinism/repeatability**: `test_deterministic_repeatability_same_input_same_output`, `test_normalization_collapses_whitespace_variants_to_same_result`.
- **Six directive adversarial Tier-0 examples**: all present as named tests in `test_qu_tier0.py`, each asserting non-collapse into the wrong intent.
- **Session semantics** (10-query/30-minute/repo-isolation/decay/stale-context-non-domination): full suite in `test_qu_session.py`.
- **Security/prompt-injection resistance**: full suite in `test_qu_security.py`.

### J. Remaining blockers

None for D8 itself. The only standing, non-blocking open item is the TAD §33 completeness-percentage metric, explicitly deferred to the Planner/Coverage calibration phase (P.2, C above) — not a D8 blocker, and not to be silently resolved when that phase begins.

**D8 — READY FOR REVIEW.** Per the directive's explicit closing instruction, D9 (Planner/Retrieval) work is not started. Stopping after D8 for review.

**Granularity note (ranking signals):** HLRD §35 lists a broader, aspirational signal set (`Semantic relevance, Structural relevance, Relationship relevance, Evidence quality, Intent relevance, Path relevance, Runtime relevance, Temporal relevance, Provider agreement, Redundancy`) with a `w1...wn` open sum; TAD §36-37 narrows this to the four V1 deterministic proxies with a closed 4-term formula. Same pattern as D8's intent-vocabulary granularity note: not a contradiction (TAD is explicitly the *technical architecture* narrowing HLRD's conceptual list to what V1 actually computes), so `codex.planner.ranking` implements exactly TAD §36-37's four signals and no others.

### R.2 Gap/contradiction audit (directive Parts 3, 6-8, 13)

Checked directly against the current `docs/TAD.md`/`docs/HLRD.md` text, not assumed:

- **QueryContract omissions (Part 3):** re-confirmed `reasoning_requirement` appears exactly once in TAD (§26, as a complexity *input* weight) and nowhere else; no later TAD section (§29-42) reads it as a standalone Planner input. `graph_version` is **not** sourced from `QueryContract` at all — TAD §20 explicitly assigns its capture to the Planner itself, fresh, at the start of DTD-03 ("At the beginning of DTD-03: graph_version = V182 is locked into RetrievalPlan.graph_version"), independent of anything Query Understanding produced. **Conclusion: category A (already correctly derivable/obtainable without touching QueryContract) — no field needs to be added to QueryContract, D8 is not reopened, no STOP.**
- **Ranking signals (Part 6):** all four V1 proxies are present in TAD §36-37, verbatim: `semantic_relevance` = BM25 over qualified names/symbols/paths/extracted query entities, normalized `[0,1]`; `structural_relevance` = `1.0` on primary-relationship match else `0.3`; `graph_proximity` = `0.9^d`; `query_constraint_match` = Jaccard similarity over paths/tags/roles/constraints (TAD's set is slightly broader than the directive's own paraphrase, which omitted "roles" — implemented per TAD's actual, broader definition). **No invention needed for the signals themselves.** The aggregation weights (`w1..w4`) are explicitly "calibration parameters" (TAD §37) with no V1 default given anywhere — this is the same class of gap as ADR-018's freshness half-life and D8's session-decay half-life: **resolved as a documented calibration point** (`RANKING_WEIGHTS = 0.25` each, equal weighting, explicitly labeled non-final), not a STOP, since the formula *shape* is fully specified and only the scalar weights are missing.
- **Budget cost estimation (Part 7):** TAD §41 gives the token-budget formula explicitly (`max_nodes = min(100, token_budget/average_node_cost)`, `max_edges = min(250, token_budget/average_edge_cost)`) but never defines `average_node_cost`/`average_edge_cost` numerically — again a formula-given/constants-missing gap, resolved as a documented calibration point. **Latency budget has no analogous formula anywhere in TAD** (§32 only describes the five-step pruning *procedure*, never how a plan's latency cost is estimated) — the only defensible non-inventive option that still lets `latency_budget_ms` function as an independent, testable budget (TAD §32 explicitly lists it as a co-equal Planner input) is to extend the *identical* average-cost-per-unit pattern TAD already specifies for tokens, with its own separately labeled calibration constants (`DEFAULT_AVERAGE_NODE_LATENCY_MS`, `DEFAULT_AVERAGE_EDGE_LATENCY_MS`). This is the single most judgment-laden call in this audit — flagged explicitly rather than silently applied; it is structural reuse of TAD's own specified pattern, not an unrelated invented cost model (e.g. no real wall-clock profiling or ML cost prediction), and remains fully swappable. **Not a STOP** under this reasoning, but recorded with maximum visibility for review.
- **Completeness percentage denominator (Part 8):** re-checked `docs/TAD.md` §33 directly — still gives only the four named thresholds (`LOW ≥50%, MEDIUM ≥75%, HIGH ≥90%, EXHAUSTIVE 100%+complete repository coverage`) with **no definition of what the percentage measures**. Re-confirmed genuinely open, not silently resolved. **Determined not to require the numeric value for D9**: `is_exhaustive_coverage()` (already implemented, `codex.coverage`) fully and deterministically satisfies the `EXHAUSTIVE` level without any percentage (it reduces to the same AND-gate as negative-query safety). For `LOW`/`MEDIUM`/`HIGH`, TAD §32's own pruning rule ("Exhaustive queries cannot be pruned below required coverage") implies these three levels are the ones *eligible* for budget pruning — D9 only needs to know *which* level was requested (already available as an enum) to decide whether pruning is permitted at all, never to compute a numeric satisfaction percentage. Retrieval's stop condition for all four levels uses HLRD §39's qualitative rule (stop when coverage requirement satisfied, or no further expected value, or providers exhausted) implemented via `codex.coverage.classify_capability_coverage()`'s deterministic AND-gate, not a percentage. **Recorded per the directive's own instruction: "OPEN — DEFERRED TO CALIBRATION," not blocking D9, not falsely marked closed.**
- **Cache scope (Part 13):** TAD §54-55 is what actually defines caching (the directive's own "TAD §46" citation names the Verification Engine section instead — the same kind of directive/document mismatch flagged repeatedly in this project's history, e.g. D8's intent-vocabulary and D7's taxonomy paraphrase; resolved as a documented interpretation, not a contradiction, since TAD §54-55 is unambiguous and internally consistent). §54's four cache-key dimensions (repository, graph_version, schema_version, policy_version) are already all present on the existing `GraphVersion` model plus `repository_id` — no new field needed. §55's "semantic contracts do not automatically invalidate merely because an unrelated file changed" principle is **not implemented as fine-grained partial invalidation in V1** — TAD defines no versioning granularity finer than the whole `GraphVersion`, so a sub-`GraphVersion` invalidation scheme would be an invented mechanism; V1's cache is correct-by-construction (a stale entry can never be returned because its key differs) at the `GraphVersion` granularity only, honestly documented as a V1 limitation rather than claimed as solved.

No genuine HLRD↔TAD contradiction found. No QueryContract field conflict. No requirement forcing D7/D8 to be reopened.

### R.3 Graph version lock verification (Part 4)

Direct inspection of `codex.graph.version.GraphVersion` and `codex.graph.{store,memory_store}` confirms the lock is safe **by construction**, requiring no new concurrency primitive: `GraphVersion` is immutable once `.published` (already enforced, `.publish()` returns a copy); `InMemoryGraphStore.__init__(self, version: GraphVersion)` binds exactly one immutable version to exactly one store instance for its entire lifetime — there is no method anywhere on `GraphReader`/`GraphStore` that could change `.version` after construction, and `IngestionPipeline.run()` (D4, unmodified) always materializes a **new** `InMemoryGraphStore` instance per call rather than mutating a previously returned one (already proven by D4's own tests: "a previously returned result is never mutated, including by a later failing run"). Therefore: **as long as the Planner and the Retrieval Engine share the exact same `GraphReader` object reference for the lifetime of one query, "silently switching versions" is structurally impossible** — there is no second version for it to switch to. No STOP; no invented locking/concurrency semantics needed.

One real, load-bearing gap found and resolved (not a STOP): **there is no "current/latest GraphReader for repository X" registry anywhere in the codebase** — `IngestionPipeline.run()` returns a fresh `IngestionResult`/`GraphStore` to its immediate caller and nothing persists a "latest version" mapping. TAD's own 18-component list has no "Graph Version Registry" component either — obtaining "the current graph for this query's repository" is an orchestration-layer concern that sits **above** the Planner (and above D9's scope), consistent with how every other cross-cutting dependency in this codebase is supplied (D1's `ProviderAdapter`, D2's `CapabilityRegistry` — all constructor/call-parameter injected, nothing globally looked up). **Resolved as an implementation detail (category 1): `plan_query()`'s public signature takes an already-selected `GraphReader` and the `IngestionResult` that produced it as explicit parameters** — the Planner's job per TAD §20 is only to *lock* (capture `.version` from) whatever reader it is handed, not to search for one. `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` telemetry (TAD §55) is recorded when a caller later re-invokes retrieval against a *different* `GraphReader.version.version_id` for the same in-flight `RetrievalPlan` — detectable structurally by comparing the locked `version_id` against whatever reader retrieval is actually asked to run against, with no external locking system required.

### R.4 Provider selection boundary (Part 5)

`codex.registry.registry.CapabilityRegistry.rank(capability, repository, *, now=None)` (D2, ADR-018-closed) already implements exactly the query-time ranking behavior TAD §31 specifies — ties broken deterministically by `provider_name`, `capability_match=0` exclusion built in via `providers_for()`. **D9 calls `rank()`/`providers_for()` directly and does not reimplement any scoring.** The ingestion-time/query-time distinction is preserved unmodified: `IngestionPipeline` (D4, untouched) still runs every usable provider so contradictory evidence reaches the graph; D9's provider selection only decides which already-ingested provider's evidence to *prioritize when ranking retrieved evidence*, never which provider gets to write to the graph — those are genuinely separate operations on separate objects (`CapabilityRegistry.evaluate()`-driven ingestion vs. `CapabilityRegistry.rank()`-driven retrieval-time preference), not collapsed.

### R.5 Retrieval execution (Part 11) — the one additive gap

Direct inspection of `codex.graph.store.GraphReader` confirms it exposes only `get_entity(canonical_id)`, `get_relationships(...)`, and `neighbors(canonical_id, ...)` — **no method exists to look up an entity by name/qualified_name, and no method enumerates all entities**. This blocks the single most basic retrieval operation TAD §35 itself lists ("entity lookup") and HLRD §33 requires ("Candidate Generation... Graph" as a source): turning `QueryContract.targets: list[str]` (free-text strings like `"authenticate"`) into starting graph entities is otherwise impossible through the existing interface. No prior D1-D8 component ever needed this (D1-D4 only *write*; D5-D8 never read the graph at all), so this was never previously discovered.

**Resolved as an additive, non-breaking extension, not a STOP and not a modification of existing D1-D8 behavior:** one new read-only method, `find_entities(*, name=None, qualified_name=None, base_type=None) -> list[RepositorySymbol]`, added to the `GraphReader` Protocol and implemented in `InMemoryGraphStore` (exact/substring string matching only — no embeddings, no fuzzy matching, fully deterministic, consistent with HLRD §34's "embeddings are optional... SHALL NOT become prerequisites"). `InMemoryGraphStore` is the only concrete implementer anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by direct search), so this is purely additive — no existing caller's behavior changes, no existing test needs updating. Documented here with maximum visibility per the "no silent architectural decisions" rule, exactly like D4's evidence-discard-bug fix and D8's `required_evidence` mapping-table addition were.

### R.6 STOP-condition disposition (Part 16)

All twelve enumerated STOP conditions checked explicitly:

1. HLRD/TAD contradiction — none found (R.2).
2. QueryContract D8/D9 conflict — none; resolved without reopening D8 (R.2).
3. GraphVersion cannot be locked consistently — resolved structurally, no invented concurrency (R.3).
4. RetrievalPlan requires unspecified fields — avoided; every `RetrievalPlan` field traces to R.1's matrix or TAD §20/29-32/34 directly (see R.7).
5. Ranking formulas undefined and required — signals are fully specified; only weights are calibration points (R.2), not blocking.
6. Budget estimation requires unspecified algorithm — token-budget formula is specified (constants missing, calibration point); latency-budget formula is extended by direct, labeled analogy to the token pattern (R.2) — the one judgment call flagged with maximum visibility, not silently resolved.
7. Completeness percentages required but undefined — re-confirmed undefined, determined non-blocking for D9 (R.2).
8. Negative-query safety unprovable via Coverage Engine — false; fully provable given an `IngestionResult` reference, supplied the same way `GraphReader` is (R.3 pattern).
9. D9 would duplicate D2 provider selection — avoided by direct reuse (R.4).
10. D9 requires LLM/SLM dependency — false; purely deterministic.
11. External implementation/code would need to be copied — false; no new external format/protocol involved in D9.
12. New ontology relationship required but unspecified — false; `DERIVED_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` (`REACHES`/`TRANSITIVE_CALLS`/`INDIRECTLY_DEPENDS_ON`) already exists and exactly covers TRACE_EXECUTION/FIND_IMPACT's needs (TAD §14, already implemented as a vocabulary, computed at query time by D9's Retrieval Engine).

**No STOP condition triggered. Proceeding to D9 implementation (Parts 17-20).**

### R.7 RetrievalPlan schema (Part 10)

Every field below traces to R.1; no speculative field added:

| Field | Traces to |
|---|---|
| `query_identity` | Implementation detail — a stable key for telemetry/cache correlation; no TAD field name given, so a plain `str` (caller-supplied or derived) |
| `graph_version` | TAD §19-20 |
| `target_entities` | Resolved from `QueryContract.targets` via `find_entities()` (R.5) |
| `relationship_types` | `QueryContract.relationship_types` (TAD §27, D8) |
| `traversal_depth` | Derived from `QueryContract.complexity`/`intent` — TAD §29 lists "traversal depth" as a Planner output without a formula; implemented as a documented, bounded default (depth 1 for lookup-shaped intents, up to a small constant ceiling for traversal-shaped intents), not unbounded |
| `provider_requirements` | `QueryContract.required_evidence` (D8) resolved via `CapabilityRegistry.rank()`/`providers_for()` (R.4) |
| `completeness_requirement` | `QueryContract.completeness_requirement` (D8, `CompletenessLevel`) |
| `budget` | `QueryContract.token_budget`/`.latency_budget_ms` (D8) plus TAD §41's derived `max_nodes`/`max_edges` |
| `ranking_strategy` | Fixed V1 constant (TAD §36-37's four-signal formula — not user/query-selectable, TAD gives no selection mechanism) |
| `stop_conditions` | HLRD §39's three-way rule, implemented via `codex.coverage` |
| `negative_query_flag` | Derived from `Intent`/query phrasing already classified by Query Understanding (TAD §34) |
| `status` | `OK` / `PRUNED` / `PLAN_BLOCKED` / `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` (TAD §32, §41's exact vocabulary) |
| `telemetry` | `PlanTelemetry` — graph_version, budget trace (original/pruned estimate, pruning occurred, reason — TAD §32's exact required fields), `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` flag (TAD §55) |

**Decision: proceed to implementation.**

## S. D9 — Query Planner/Retrieval: Cross-DTD Audit After Implementation — 2026-08-31

Package: `src/codex/planner/` (`models.py`, `provider_selection.py`, `budget.py`, `retrieval.py`, `ranking.py`, `cache.py`, `mss.py`, `planner.py`, `__init__.py`). Additive extension: `codex.graph.store.GraphReader.find_entities()` / `codex.graph.memory_store.InMemoryGraphStore.find_entities()` (§R.5). Tests: `tests/test_planner_{planning,graph_version,provider_selection,budget,completeness,negative_queries,ranking,mss,cache,boundaries,retrieval}.py`, `tests/planner_fixtures.py`, plus 6 new `find_entities` tests appended to `tests/test_graph_store.py`. 92 new planner tests + 6 new graph-store tests = 98 new tests. 100% coverage on all nine new `codex.planner` modules; `codex.graph.{store,memory_store}` unaffected in behavior (additive only, all pre-existing tests pass unmodified). Validated in a fresh venv: `ruff check src tests` clean, `mypy src` clean (54 source files), full suite 522 passed.

### A. Requirements implemented in full

`plan_query()` (DTD-03, TAD §29): graph-version lock (TAD §20), provider selection via unmodified D2 `CapabilityRegistry.rank()` (TAD §31), budget-aware five-step pruning (TAD §32) with `PLAN_BLOCKED`/`PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` distinguished per TAD §32/§41, negative-query safety via unmodified `codex.coverage` (TAD §34), completeness-level propagation (TAD §33). `execute_query()` (DTD-04, TAD §35): deterministic bounded traversal, TAD §36-37's four ranking signals with exact literal constants (`1.0`/`0.3`, `0.9^d`), TAD §39-40's MSS construction and bounded SOURCE_CONTEXT expansion (2 cycles/50 nodes, exact), TAD §42's `EvidencePackage`. TAD §54-55's query-level cache, keyed exactly on repository/graph_version/schema_version/policy_version/query-identity. `GraphVersionMismatchError` proves the graph-version lock structurally (§R.3's "holding the reference is the lock" claim is now enforced, not just asserted).

### B. Requirements partially implemented

None. Every R.1-traced item has a corresponding implementation and dedicated test category; scope exclusions (C below) are complete non-implementations honestly deferred, not partial stubs.

### C. Requirements intentionally deferred (not blocking D9)

- **TAD §33 LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH completeness percentage** — the denominator remains genuinely undefined in TAD; D9 propagates the requested `CompletenessLevel` and refuses to prune `EXHAUSTIVE` below required coverage (the one level with a full quantitative check, `codex.coverage.is_exhaustive_coverage`), without computing a numeric percentage for the other three, per R.2's reasoning.
- **Fine-grained (sub-`GraphVersion`) cache invalidation** — TAD defines no versioning granularity finer than the whole `GraphVersion`; V1's cache correctness is by construction at that granularity only (`cache.py`'s own docstring).
- **Actual source-text retrieval for `EvidencePackage.source_context`** — carries `SourceLocation`-bearing `RepositorySymbol`s, not fetched file content; no D1-D8 provider exposes arbitrary file-content-at-revision reads.
- **A "Graph Version Registry" / "current version for repository X" lookup** — deliberately not built; obtaining the `GraphReader` to plan against is an orchestration-layer concern above D9, matching how every other cross-cutting dependency in this codebase is supplied (explicit injection, never global lookup).

### D. New ambiguities discovered during implementation

None beyond the two already flagged pre-implementation (latency-budget cost-model extension by analogy, R.2; ranking-weight calibration point, R.2). No new HLRD/TAD gap surfaced only during coding.

### E. Cross-document contradictions found

None. One directive/document citation mismatch confirmed and resolved as a documented interpretation, not a contradiction: the directive's "TAD §46" for cache architecture actually names the Verification Engine section; the real cache sections are TAD §54-55 (R.2) — the same class of paraphrase mismatch flagged repeatedly across this project's history (D7's taxonomy, D8's intent vocabulary).

### F. Silent implementation choices avoided / documented interpretations made

Per directive Phase 19's classification: **implementation detail** (category 1) — `_BASE_DEPTH_BY_INTENT` (TAD §29 names "traversal depth" as a Planner output with no formula), `DEFAULT_AVERAGE_NODE_COST_TOKENS`/`DEFAULT_AVERAGE_EDGE_COST_TOKENS` (TAD §41 gives the formula, not the constants — ADR-018 precedent), `DEFAULT_LATENCY_MS_PER_DEPTH_LEVEL` (no TAD formula at all for latency; extended from the token pattern by direct, labeled analogy — the single most judgment-laden call in D9, R.2/R.6), `RANKING_WEIGHTS` equal 0.25 each (TAD §37: "weights are calibration parameters," no default given), `query_identity` field (no TAD name given; a deterministic content hash). **Documented interpretation** (category 2) — `query_constraint_match`'s vacuous-case rule (no constraints requested = trivially satisfied, `1.0`); `RetrievalPlan.constraints` added as a field beyond R.7's original checklist, needed to make `query_constraint_match` meaningfully exercised rather than trivially `1.0` on every real query (traces cleanly to `QueryContract.constraints`, TAD §27, flowing into TAD §36's own signal — not a speculative addition, a necessary wiring of two already-approved contracts). No category-3 (ADR-required) or category-4 (STOP) decisions were needed.

### G. Security concerns identified and how addressed

`QueryContract` is treated as trusted, already-validated structured input (D8's own boundary) — the planner never re-parses query text; `plan_query`/`execute_query` derive every decision from typed `QueryContract`/`RetrievalPlan` fields only. `resolve_targets`/`find_entities` perform deterministic substring matching with no code execution, no embeddings, no external calls. `execute_query` refuses (`GraphVersionMismatchError`) rather than silently executing against a mismatched graph snapshot — a concurrency-safety property, not just a functional one.

### H. Dependency-boundary findings

None. `tests/test_planner_boundaries.py` programmatically parses every module's imports via `ast` and asserts none of `codex.llm`, `codex.slm`, `codex.verification`, `codex.query_understanding.slm`, or the behavioral provider submodules (`codex.provider.contract`/`.git_adapter`/`.scip_adapter`/`.codeql_adapter`/`.scip`) are imported anywhere in `codex.planner`. A regex check confirms no hard-coded provider-name branching exists. A structural check confirms `select_providers()` is a thin pass-through to `CapabilityRegistry.rank()` with no local scoring function. `RetrievalPlan` carries no `answer`/`verification_status`/`claims`/`llm_response`-shaped field.

### I. Tests proving each architectural invariant

- **Graph-version lock**: `test_graph_version_is_captured_into_the_plan`, `test_concurrent_graph_update_does_not_change_an_in_flight_plan`, `test_execute_query_against_a_different_version_raises_concurrent_update`.
- **No provider-selection duplication**: `test_select_providers_reuses_registry_rank_not_a_new_algorithm`, `test_ingestion_time_runs_every_usable_provider_query_time_only_ranks`.
- **Budget pruning order + EXHAUSTIVE refusal**: full suite in `test_planner_budget.py`, including both blocking paths (latency, token).
- **Negative-query safety via `codex.coverage`, never bypassed**: full suite in `test_planner_negative_queries.py`, including a hand-crafted `PARTIAL` cohort and a stale-but-COMPLETE cohort proving staleness is not conflated with completeness.
- **Ranking determinism/tie-breaking/each signal**: full suite in `test_planner_ranking.py`.
- **MSS bounds (2 cycles/50 nodes) and PARTIAL reporting**: full suite in `test_planner_mss.py`, including a frontier-entity-skipped-once-cycle-is-full test.
- **Cache key isolation on all four TAD §54 dimensions**: full suite in `test_planner_cache.py`.
- **No LLM/SLM/verification/provider-specific dependency**: `test_planner_boundaries.py`.

### J. Remaining blockers

None for D9 itself. Standing, non-blocking open items carried forward unchanged: TAD §33's completeness-percentage metric (deferred to Planner/Coverage calibration, as it already was after D8); the latency-budget cost-model analogy (R.2/R.6) remains the one item most worth a human calibration pass if/when real latency data becomes available, but does not block D9's correctness today.

**D9 — READY FOR REVIEW.** Per the directive's explicit closing instruction, D10 (LLM Gateway/Answer Generation/Verification Engine) work is not started. Stopping after D9 for review.

Audit-only per explicit instruction: no production code was written or modified in this phase. Read directly (not assumed from prior reports): `docs/HLRD.md` §26, §41-46; `docs/TAD.md` §19-20, §29-55; `docs/architecture-reconciliation.md`; `docs/architecture-conformance-audit.md` §A-S; `PROGRESS.md`; and inspected directly: `src/codex/planner/*`, `src/codex/ingestion/pipeline.py` (step ordering), `src/codex/reconciliation/reconciler.py`, `src/codex/evidence/model.py`, `src/codex/graph/version.py`.

### T.1 Phase A — D9 Closure Audit (15 items)

| # | Item | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | QueryContract → RetrievalPlan → EvidencePackage traceable | IMPLEMENTED | `plan_query()`/`execute_query()` (`planner.py`); every `RetrievalPlan`/`EvidencePackage` field traced in §R.7/§S.F |
| 2 | graph_version immutable, consistent planning→retrieval | IMPLEMENTED | `GraphVersion.publish()` is copy-on-write (§R.3); `execute_query` raises `GraphVersionMismatchError` on any `graph.version.version_id` mismatch against `plan.graph_version.version_id` — verified structurally, not just asserted |
| 3 | Provider selection delegated to D2, no duplicate ranking | IMPLEMENTED | `provider_selection.select_providers()` calls `registry.rank()`/`.providers_for()` only; `test_provider_selection_module_only_calls_registry_rank_or_providers_for` proves no local `rank()`/`score()` function exists in the module |
| 4 | Ranking follows canonical V1 deterministic proxies exactly | IMPLEMENTED | `ranking.py`'s `bm25_scores`/`structural_relevance`/`graph_proximity`/`query_constraint_match` match TAD §36's literal constants (`1.0`/`0.3`, `0.9^d`) verbatim; re-verified against the current `docs/TAD.md` text in this audit, not assumed |
| 5 | Budget pruning cannot silently violate EXHAUSTIVE completeness | IMPLEMENTED | `plan_query()` checks `is_exhaustive` before every pruning step (depth reduction, relationship-type removal) and returns `PLAN_BLOCKED` instead — `test_exhaustive_query_is_blocked_not_silently_pruned_on_latency`/`_on_token_truncation` |
| 6 | Negative-query planning delegates to Coverage Engine | IMPLEMENTED | `plan_query()` calls `codex.coverage.engine.evaluate_negative_query_coverage()` directly; no local re-implementation |
| 7 | MSS expansion remains bounded | IMPLEMENTED | `mss.py`'s `MAX_EXPANSION_CYCLES=2`, `MAX_NODES_PER_CYCLE=50` — TAD §40's exact literal bounds, tested including the frontier-entity-skipped-once-full case |
| 8 | Evidence budgets derived from token budget, not arbitrary independent limits | IMPLEMENTED, with one clarification | `max_nodes`/`max_edges` (TAD §41's own "Dynamic Evidence Budget") are derived **purely** from `token_budget` via `token_derived_ceiling()`, exactly matching TAD §41's formula shape. `traversal_depth`'s separate latency-derived ceiling (`latency_derived_depth_ceiling()`) is a *different* knob — it bounds traversal depth, not evidence volume — and is TAD's own named co-equal budget input (§32) with no formula given at all, resolved as a calibration-point extension (§R.2/§R.6, flagged with maximum visibility, not silent). Re-confirmed: it is not an "arbitrary independent" evidence-volume limit; it does not compete with or override the token-derived ceiling. |
| 9 | Retrieval cache keys include every required invalidation dimension | IMPLEMENTED | `cache_key_for()` includes `repository_id`, `graph_version_id`, `schema_version`, `policy_version` (TAD §54's exact four) plus `query_identity` (a deterministic `QueryContract` content hash) |
| 10 | No planner/retrieval transitive dependency on LLM/SLM/verification | IMPLEMENTED | AST-verified (`test_planner_boundaries.py`); re-confirmed structurally impossible today since `codex.llm`/`codex.slm`/`codex.verification` do not exist anywhere in the codebase yet — there is nothing to transitively depend on |

A future Verification/Answer stage wanting to *explain* a `DISPUTED`/`CONTRADICTED` claim (HLRD §43: "explain conflict") would need the contradicting `Evidence` objects themselves, not just the `contradiction_score` float and the raw id strings already present in `CanonicalRelationship.contradicting_evidence_ids`. **Not blocking**: the ids are already present in the package (nothing is lost), and D10 can resolve them itself given the same `EvidenceStore` reference (matching D9's own explicit-injection precedent, §R.3) — the same pattern already used throughout D1-D9. Recommended, non-blocking fix: extend `collect_evidence()`/`execute_query()` to also resolve `contradicting_evidence_ids` into `EvidencePackage.evidence` before D10.2 needs it, so the package is self-sufficient per TAD §42's own framing ("the LLM's repository context boundary"). |
