| 13 | Entity resolution and reconciliation semantics preserved | IMPLEMENTED | Re-verified directly: `IngestionPipeline.run()` calls `self._reconcile_relationships(...)` (line 218) **before** `_materialize_store(...)` (line 220) which produces the `graph_store` D9 reads — D9 structurally cannot see pre-resolution/pre-reconciliation state, it only ever sees the fully-resolved, fully-reconciled graph |
| 14 | GraphVersion and provider-version provenance survive into EvidencePackage | IMPLEMENTED | `EvidencePackage.graph_version: GraphVersion` carries the full object including `provider_versions: dict[str, str]` (TAD §19) unmodified; each individual `Evidence` record also independently carries `provider`/`provider_version` |
| 15 | No D9 implementation silently introduced semantics absent from HLRD/TAD | IMPLEMENTED | Every non-trivial choice already classified in §S.F (implementation detail vs. documented interpretation); re-confirmed no category-3/4 (ADR-required/STOP) decision was silently resolved |

**D9 closure disposition:** 14/15 IMPLEMENTED, 1/15 PARTIAL (item 11), **not blocking D10** — reasoning given above. No item is MISSING, CONTRADICTED, or AMBIGUOUS.

### T.2 Phase B — D10 Contract Reconstruction

Reconstructed directly from `docs/TAD.md` §43-52 and `docs/HLRD.md` §41-43 (re-read in full for this audit, not assumed):

| Responsibility | Authoritative source | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| LLM boundary | HLRD §41 ("non-negotiable... LLM SHALL NOT be treated as an authoritative source of repository truth"); TAD §30 (Planner boundary, analogous), TAD §43 | LLM receives `Query + EvidencePackage + ResponseContract`; "does not receive unrestricted repository access" |
| Answer generation | TAD §43-44 | One structured LLM generation pass; JSON output `{answer, claims[]}` |
| Claim extraction | TAD §44 | Claims are part of the SAME structured output, not a separate extraction pass — "Do NOT build a separate extraction model" (directive Phase C.1) matches TAD's own single-schema design |
| Claim schema | TAD §44-45 | `{subject, predicate, object, claim_type}`; `claim_type ∈ {FACT, DERIVED, INFERENCE, UNKNOWN}` (§45) |
| Entailment | TAD §46-47 | DTD-05 pipeline: claim extraction validation → evidence mapping → entailment → contradiction detection → coverage verification → confidence → answer decision. Deterministic entailment: direct edge matching, path existence, bounded graph traversal, set membership, type hierarchy. Unsupported semantic assertions → `UNRESOLVED` |
| Contradiction handling | TAD §49 | `CONTRADICTED → REMOVE CLAIM → RE-SYNTHESIZE`, never a speculative rewrite. Maximum V1 re-synthesis: 1 |
| Verification score | TAD §48 | `V = 0.35 evidence_support + 0.20 evidence_quality + 0.15 evidence_independence + 0.10 coverage + 0.10 freshness + 0.10 provider_authority`; `V = min(V, 0.50)` if "significant contradictory evidence"; "weak contradiction may receive a small penalty" |
| Verification taxonomy | TAD §50 (canonical, 6-value) | `VERIFIED, PARTIALLY_VERIFIED, QUALIFIED, DISPUTED, INCONCLUSIVE, REJECTED` — already the single canonical internal model per the 2026-08-30 reconciliation (C-3); HLRD §42's 4-value labels and TAD §50's own routing-bucket table are **derived views**, not competing taxonomies |
| Re-synthesis | TAD §44 (invalid schema), §49 (contradicted claim) | Maximum 1 re-synthesis cycle (TAD §49's explicit number); whether the schema-invalid and contradiction-triggered paths share one counter or are independent is **not stated** — flagged in T.4 |
| Hallucination enforcement | HLRD §41 (Verification Layer "is the enforcement mechanism against hallucination"); TAD §47 (`UNRESOLVED` default) | Any claim without deterministic support defaults to `UNRESOLVED`, never silently `VERIFIED` |
| Graph-version handling | TAD §20 ("flows through DTD-04 → EvidencePackage → LLM → DTD-05"); TAD §55 | The locked `graph_version` must reach DTD-05 unchanged; re-reading "the current graph" during verification would violate §20 |
| Security boundaries | HLRD §41; TAD §30, §43 ("does not receive unrestricted repository access") | See T.5 |
| Audit/telemetry | TAD §32's telemetry pattern (already implemented in D9, `PlanTelemetry`), TAD §55's `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` | No D10-specific telemetry struct defined in TAD yet — an implementation detail for D10, following the D9 `PlanTelemetry` precedent |
| Token/latency budget | TAD §27 (already on `QueryContract`), §41-42 | D10 must respect the same budgets already carried through `RetrievalPlan`/`EvidencePackage` — no new budget concept introduced |
| Maximum retry/re-synthesis count | TAD §49 | Exactly 1 |

**Pipeline shape** (matches the directive's own diagram, TAD §43+§46 combined): `EvidencePackage → LLM Gateway → Structured Answer + Claims → Verification Engine (entailment/contradiction/coverage/confidence) → Verification Result → optional ONE re-synthesis → Final Answer/Abstention`. TAD's own component list (§6, D-phase's first audit) already keeps `LLM Gateway` and `Verification Engine` as two distinct components (#14, #15) — confirms the directive's "do not collapse these components" instruction matches TAD's own decomposition, not an external addition.

### T.3 Phase C — Verification of the Previously-Agreed D10 Decisions

1. **Claim extraction** — confirmed consistent with TAD §44 (single schema, no separate extraction model). No contradiction.
2. **Deterministic entailment** — confirmed consistent with TAD §47's literal list (direct edge matching, path existence, bounded traversal, set membership, type hierarchy — "deterministic role/name rules where explicitly defined" is the directive's paraphrase of "type hierarchy"/"set membership," not a new mechanism). `UNRESOLVED` default for unsupported semantic claims matches TAD §47 exactly. No contradiction.
3. **Contradicted claims** — TAD §49's `REMOVE CLAIM → RE-SYNTHESIZE` confirmed. The directive's elaboration ("if removal makes the answer incomplete/incoherent: retain only supported claims, mark the final result appropriately, annotate that the removed assertion lacked supporting evidence") is **not literally present in TAD's text** but is a non-contradictory operational elaboration consistent with HLRD §43's Answer Policy (`CONTRADICTED → explain conflict / downgrade confidence / possibly abstain`) — classified as a documented interpretation (category 2), not a silent invention, and not a contradiction.
4. **Contradiction penalty** — TAD §48's own text uses **qualitative** language only: "If **significant** contradictory evidence exists: V = min(V, 0.50). **Weak** contradiction may receive a small penalty." **Finding**: the directive's specific numeric thresholds ("confidence > 0.6" for significant, "<0.4" for weak) do **not** appear anywhere in `docs/TAD.md`, `docs/HLRD.md`, `docs/architecture-reconciliation.md`, or the conformance audit — confirmed by direct text search, not assumed. This is a **directive-vs-document mismatch**, the same recurring pattern flagged in every prior phase (D7's taxonomy, D8's intent vocabulary, D9's cache section-citation): the directive states these thresholds as "previously agreed," but no prior commit in this repository's history establishes them. **This does not block the audit** (Phase A-F require no code), but **does need resolution before D10.5 (contradiction handling) is implemented** — see T.7/Open Architectural Decisions. The qualitative rule itself (significant → cap at 0.50; weak → small penalty) is not in dispute, only the exact numeric boundary.
5. **Verification taxonomy** — confirmed: TAD §50 already contains both mapping tables (HLRD presentation mapping, pipeline routing bucket) verbatim, and TAD §50 states they will be "implemented as tested pure functions when the Verification Engine is built, never re-derived ad hoc." No new taxonomy needed; nothing to build here except the two small pure mapping functions themselves as part of D10.6.
6. **Graph version** — confirmed: TAD §20's explicit propagation chain (`DTD-03 → ... → DTD-04 → EvidencePackage → LLM → DTD-05`) already names DTD-05 (Verification) as a recipient of the same locked `graph_version`. D9's `EvidencePackage.graph_version` already carries the full, immutable `GraphVersion` object, so D10 has everything needed to avoid re-reading "the current graph" — this is an implementation obligation for D10 (never construct a fresh `GraphReader` lookup inside Verification), not a gap in what D9 already produced.

### T.4 Phase D — Cross-DTD Contradiction Analysis

Checked all 15 named categories directly against the current documents:

1. **Conflicting verification taxonomies** — none; TAD §50 is singular and canonical, HLRD §42 is an explicitly-marked derived view (reconciled 2026-08-30, C-3).
2. **Conflicting EvidenceStatus vs. VerificationStatus semantics** — **naming collision, not a contradiction**: `EvidenceStatus.DISPUTED` (TAD §18, relationship-level, already computed by the implemented Reconciliation Engine) and the canonical `VerificationStatus`'s `DISPUTED` (TAD §50, claim-level, not yet implemented) share a literal enum value name but are different types operating at different levels (edge vs. claim) with different producers (Reconciliation Engine vs. Verification Engine). This was already implicitly acknowledged during the Reconciliation phase ("TAD §50 governs the much later Verification Engine, untouched"). **Flagged for D10 implementation discipline**: `codex.verification`'s claim-level status must never be conflated with, or directly assigned from, a `CanonicalRelationship.status` of the same name — it is one *input signal* to deterministic entailment, not a shortcut answer. Not a document contradiction; a naming clash worth a code comment when D10 is built.
3. **Conflicting confidence formulas** — none; TAD §48 (Verification Confidence, claim-level) and TAD §37 (Ranking, entity/edge-level) are different formulas for different purposes, never presented as interchangeable.
4. **Conflicting contradiction formulas** — TAD §38 (Evidence Contradiction, already implemented in `codex.reconciliation`, relationship-level `contradiction_score`) and TAD §48's "significant/weak contradictory evidence" (claim-level, Verification Confidence penalty) are **related but distinct**: §48's V-formula penalty is expected to *consume* §38's already-computed `contradiction_score` (via the `CanonicalRelationship`s the claim's entailment maps to) rather than recompute contradiction from scratch — this is a reuse relationship, not a conflict, and is the natural reading of TAD §51's traceability chain. No contradiction; a design recommendation for D10.3/D10.4 to reuse `codex.reconciliation`'s existing `contradiction_score` rather than reinvent it.
5. **Conflicting graph-version semantics** — none (T.3 item 6).
6. **Conflicting completeness semantics** — none beyond the already-tracked, non-blocking TAD §33 percentage-denominator gap (T.8).
7. **Conflicting claim schema** — none; TAD §44-45 is the only claim schema defined anywhere.
8. **Conflicting LLM/planner boundary** — none; TAD §30 (Planner) and TAD §43 (LLM Gateway) are consistent, non-overlapping boundary statements, both already enforced/enforceable (D9's boundary tests; D10's forthcoming ones).
9. **Conflicting re-synthesis rules** — **one genuine open question, not a contradiction**: TAD names "Maximum V1 re-synthesis: 1" once (§49, contradiction-triggered) and separately says invalid/missing claims trigger `RESYNTHESIS` (§44, schema-triggered) without stating whether these share one counter. Both statements are individually clear; their *interaction* is unspecified. Flagged in Open Architectural Decisions (T.7) — recommended resolution given, not silently assumed.
10. **Conflicting evidence provenance requirements** — none; TAD §51's traceability chain is singular and consistent with every already-implemented D1-D9 provenance field.
11. **Conflicting retry limits** — same as #9; one number given, one interaction question open.
12. **Conflicting budget semantics** — none; T.1 item 8 already resolves the token-vs-latency-budget relationship as complementary, not conflicting.
13. **Conflicting meaning of PARTIAL** — checked directly: `CapabilityCoverage.PARTIAL` (TAD §17/§34, provider-run-level), `EvidencePackage.partial` (D9's own field, package-level "did not fully satisfy the plan"), and TAD §50's `PARTIALLY_VERIFIED` (claim-level) are three distinct concepts at three distinct levels, never asserted equivalent anywhere, and each already has a single unambiguous meaning within its own level. No contradiction; a terminology overlap worth naming explicitly in D10's own module docstrings (as `codex.coverage`/D9 already do for their own level).
14. **Conflicting meaning of UNRESOLVED/INCONCLUSIVE** — checked directly: `EvidenceStatus.UNRESOLVED` (TAD §18, relationship-level: "not yet reconciled" or "missing-entity endpoint," already implemented) vs. TAD §47's entailment `UNRESOLVED` (claim-level: "no deterministic rule could resolve this claim") vs. `NegativeQueryCoverage.INCONCLUSIVE` (TAD §34, coverage-level: "absence not trustworthy," already implemented) are three distinct, non-conflicting concepts at three levels — same pattern as #13, no contradiction.
15. **D9 implementation accidentally anticipating/constraining D10** — checked: D9 introduces `PlanStatus`, `NegativeQueryCoverage`, `CapabilityCoverage`, `EvidenceStatus` reuse, and `EvidencePackage.partial`/`.limitations` — none of these overlap with or pre-empt TAD §50's canonical verification taxonomy; `EvidencePackage.partial` is a boolean coverage-completeness flag, not a verification verdict, and nothing in D9 assigns a `VERIFIED`/`DISPUTED`/etc. value anywhere. No constraint found.

**No genuine HLRD↔TAD contradiction found anywhere in this sweep.** Two open questions surfaced (naming collision at #2, re-synthesis-counter interaction at #9/#11) — both are gaps requiring a small, explicit decision before the relevant D10 sub-phase, not contradictions requiring a STOP.

### T.5 Phase E — D10 Security Boundary Audit

No D10 code exists yet to AST-audit directly (`codex.llm`/`codex.verification` do not exist in the codebase — confirmed by directory listing). This section states the invariants D10's eventual implementation must satisfy, each traced to its authoritative source and to the existing precedent that already enforces the analogous invariant one layer up:

- **Repository text is untrusted data** — HLRD §41 ("LLM SHALL NOT be treated as an authoritative source of repository truth"); precedent already set by D8's `QueryContract` security tests (`test_qu_security.py`: user query text never modifies structured fields) and D9's boundary tests.
- **EvidencePackage is data, not instructions** — TAD §43 ("LLM receives Query + EvidencePackage + ResponseContract... does not receive unrestricted repository access"); D10 must construct the LLM prompt so that every EvidencePackage field (entity names, evidence text, source locations — all ultimately repository-derived strings) is passed as data content, never concatenated into instruction-bearing prompt segments the model could be steered by.
- **Retrieved source code cannot override system/developer policy** — same principle, applies specifically to `EvidencePackage.source_context` (`RepositorySymbol.source_location`-bearing entities) once real source-snippet retrieval is added (currently deferred, §S.C) — repository file content is exactly the class of string HLRD §41 forbids treating as authoritative.
- **Repository comments/docstrings cannot instruct the LLM to ignore verification** — same as above; also structurally reinforced by the fact that Verification (TAD §46-47) is deterministic and runs *after* the LLM call, over the LLM's own structured claims against the (separately, deterministically retrieved) evidence — a comment cannot talk its way past a check it never participates in.
- **LLM output cannot modify EvidencePackage** — architectural: `EvidencePackage` is D9's output, constructed before the LLM is ever invoked (TAD §43's own input ordering); D10 must not design any mutation path from LLM response back into the package.
- **LLM output cannot modify GraphVersion** — same; `GraphVersion` is locked by D9 (T.1 item 2) before D10 exists in the call chain; D10 must only *read* `EvidencePackage.graph_version`, never construct or request a new one.
- **LLM output cannot create evidence** — architectural: `Evidence` records only ever originate from `ProviderAdapter.normalize()` (D1) via `IngestionPipeline` (D4); D10's claims are typed `{subject, predicate, object, claim_type}` (TAD §45), never an `Evidence`-shaped object, and must never be upserted into `EvidenceStore`/`GraphStore`.
- **LLM output cannot promote a claim to VERIFIED** — TAD §46's own pipeline ordering (claim extraction → ... → confidence → answer decision) places verification-state assignment strictly after and independent of the LLM call; the LLM's own `claim_type` field (`FACT`/`DERIVED`/`INFERENCE`/`UNKNOWN`, TAD §45) is the model's *claim about itself*, never the verification verdict — D10 must keep these two enums structurally separate (also T.4 item 2's naming-collision discipline).
- **Verification is deterministic and authoritative over LLM assertions** — TAD §47 ("Complex semantic assertions default to UNRESOLVED unless deterministic rules exist") and HLRD §41 (Verification Layer as "the enforcement mechanism against hallucination") both state this directly.
- **The LLM cannot choose files/providers/evidence outside the supplied EvidencePackage** — TAD §43's closed input list (`Query + EvidencePackage + ResponseContract`) and the already-established Planner precedent (TAD §30, D9 §R.4: "the planner determines retrieval scope... never asks 'what files should I look at?'") extends identically to the LLM Gateway: it receives a pre-selected, bounded package and has no tool-call/file-access surface in V1's design (TAD §43 lists no such capability).
- **The planner cannot ask the LLM which files to inspect** — already true and tested today (D9 §R.11, `test_no_llm_or_slm_model_dependency_imported`, `test_no_module_imports_forbidden_dependencies`); re-confirmed as still true with no code change in this audit phase.
- **The verifier cannot ask the LLM whether its own claim is true** — TAD §47's deterministic-only entailment list contains no LLM invocation; TAD §46's pipeline diagram never routes back to the LLM Gateway from Verification except via the single explicit RE-SYNTHESIZE path (§49), which asks for a *new answer*, not a self-judgment of the old one.

No violation exists today (no D10 code exists). Every invariant traces to an authoritative source or an already-enforced precedent one layer up. **D10.9 (security/boundary tests) must include an AST-based import-boundary test for `codex.llm`/`codex.verification` matching the `codex.planner`/`codex.query_understanding` precedent, plus a prompt-construction test proving EvidencePackage content is never string-concatenated into a system/instruction-role prompt segment.**

### T.6 Phase F — D10 Failure Matrix

| Failure | Internal state | Verification state | Re-synthesis allowed? | Final answer allowed? | User receives | Telemetry event |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LLM timeout | No structured output | N/A (no claims to verify) | No (not a schema/contradiction failure) | No | Abstention | `LLM_TIMEOUT` |
| LLM malformed JSON | Schema validation failed | N/A | Yes (TAD §44, counts toward the 1 max) | Only if re-synthesis succeeds | Answer, or abstention if retry also fails | `RESYNTHESIS_TRIGGERED` (reason=malformed_json) |
| Missing claims array | Schema validation failed | N/A | Yes (TAD §44) | Only if re-synthesis succeeds | Answer, or abstention | `RESYNTHESIS_TRIGGERED` (reason=missing_claims) |
| Invalid claim schema (per-claim) | Schema validation failed | N/A | Yes (TAD §44) | Only if re-synthesis succeeds | Answer, or abstention | `RESYNTHESIS_TRIGGERED` (reason=invalid_claim) |
| Unsupported predicate (not in `RelationshipType`) | Claim un-mappable to canonical ontology | `UNRESOLVED` (per-claim; TAD §47 default) | No (not a schema/contradiction trigger by itself) | Yes, with that claim excluded/qualified | Qualified answer | `CLAIM_UNRESOLVED` (reason=unsupported_predicate) |
| No evidence for a claim | Entailment finds no matching `CanonicalRelationship`/`Evidence` | `UNRESOLVED` (TAD §47) | No | Yes, qualified | Qualified answer or abstention if it's the only claim | `CLAIM_UNRESOLVED` (reason=no_evidence) |
| Insufficient evidence (found but below completeness) | Coverage below `completeness_requirement` | `PARTIALLY_VERIFIED`/`QUALIFIED` (TAD §50) | No | Yes, qualified | Qualified answer, limitations noted | `COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT` |
| Contradictory evidence | `CanonicalRelationship.contradiction_score`/`.status` indicates conflict | `DISPUTED` (significant) or penalized `VERIFIED`/`PARTIALLY_VERIFIED` (weak, T.3 item 4's open threshold) | Yes if significant (TAD §49, max 1) | Yes after removal, or with explicit qualification if re-synthesis exhausted | Qualified answer noting the removed/contested claim | `CLAIM_CONTRADICTED`, `RESYNTHESIS_TRIGGERED` (reason=contradiction) |
| Stale graph (concurrent update mid-query) | `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` (TAD §55, already implemented in D9 as `GraphVersionMismatchError`) | Unaffected — verification still runs against the originally locked version | No | Yes, against the locked (now possibly outdated) version | Answer (reproducible against locked version), telemetry-visible staleness | `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` (already emitted by D9) |
| Stale evidence (old `Evidence.freshness`) | `freshness` factor lowers `V` (TAD §48) | Lower confidence, possibly `QUALIFIED` | No | Yes, qualified | Qualified answer | `LOW_FRESHNESS` |
| Provider failure (upstream, at ingestion time) | Already classified by `codex.coverage` (`FAILED`) | Reduces `coverage`/`evidence_quality` factors | No (D10 concern, not a D10-triggered retry) | Yes, qualified/abstain per coverage severity | Qualified answer or abstention (negative-query safety, TAD §34, already enforced in D9) | Already `PROVIDER_FAILURE`-classified upstream (D1-D4) |
| Partial EvidenceCohort | Already classified (`CapabilityCoverage.PARTIAL`) | Lowers `coverage` factor | No | Yes, qualified | Qualified answer, limitations noted | Already `COHORT_PARTIAL`-classified upstream |
| Incomplete negative-query coverage | `NegativeQueryCoverage.INCONCLUSIVE` (already computed by D9) | `INCONCLUSIVE` (TAD §50) — never `FALSE` | No | Yes, as an explicit "cannot determine" | Abstention-shaped qualified answer, never a false negative | Already emitted by D9 (`plan.negative_query_result`) |
| Token budget exhaustion | `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`/`PLAN_BLOCKED` at D9, or exceeded during LLM generation | N/A if pre-empted at D9; `INCONCLUSIVE` if exhausted mid-D10 | No | No if D9 already blocked; qualified/abstain if exhausted in D10 | Abstention or qualified answer | Already `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`/`PLAN_BLOCKED` (D9) or new `TOKEN_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` (D10) |
| Latency budget exhaustion | Same pattern as token budget | Same | No | Same | Same | `LATENCY_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` |
| Re-synthesis failure (2nd attempt also invalid/contradicted) | Retry budget exhausted (TAD §49's "Maximum V1 re-synthesis: 1") | `REJECTED` or `INCONCLUSIVE` depending on failure type | No (budget exhausted) | No, or abstention-only | Abstention/rejection, explicit reason given | `RESYNTHESIS_EXHAUSTED` |
| Repeated invalid LLM output (both attempts malformed) | Same as above | N/A | No | No | Abstention | `RESYNTHESIS_EXHAUSTED` |
| Security/prompt-injection content in repository | Detected structurally never — the content is inert data (T.5); no special-case "detection" state needed since it's never interpreted as instructions | Unaffected — verification runs on structured claims only | No | Yes, normally, if the claims are otherwise valid/verified | Normal answer; the injection attempt itself never surfaces as behavior | None needed beyond normal claim-level events (proven inert by construction, not by runtime detection) |
| Conflicting evidence from independent providers | `CanonicalRelationship.contradiction_score` (TAD §38, already computed, independence-aware) feeds the `evidence_independence` factor (TAD §48) | `DISPUTED` if the conflict is significant across independent sources | Yes if triggered (TAD §49) | Yes, qualified, noting cross-provider disagreement | Qualified answer | `CLAIM_CONTRADICTED` (reason=cross_provider) |

### T.7 Open Architectural Decisions

1. **Contradiction-penalty numeric thresholds** (T.3 item 4) — TAD §48 gives only qualitative "significant"/"weak" language; the directive's `>0.6`/`<0.4` values are not found in any authoritative document. **Recommendation**: resolve as a documented calibration point at D10.4/D10.5 time, following the exact ADR-018/D8-decay/D9-ranking-weight precedent (a clearly-labeled, swappable default, not a claimed-final value) — `0.6`/`0.4` are reasonable defaults consistent with the directive's stated intent and can be adopted as such **if the user confirms this is acceptable**, rather than silently invented.
2. **Re-synthesis counter interaction** (T.4 items 9/11) — does the schema-invalid path (TAD §44) and the contradiction path (TAD §49) share one global "maximum 1 re-synthesis" counter, or does each failure class get its own budget of 1? **Recommendation**: a single shared counter (one re-synthesis attempt total per answer, regardless of trigger reason) is the more conservative, TAD-consistent reading (TAD §49 says "Maximum V1 re-synthesis: 1" without qualifying it per failure type), and avoids an unbounded-retry loop where a malformed-then-contradicted-then-malformed-again cycle could otherwise consume multiple LLM calls.
3. **`ResponseContract`'s exact structure** (T.2) — TAD §43 names it as an LLM Gateway input but never defines its fields separately from §44's `{answer, claims[]}` schema. **Recommendation**: treat `ResponseContract` as the formal JSON Schema descriptor whose required shape TAD §44 already gives (i.e., the schema-as-artifact the Gateway constructs and passes to constrain generation) — not a new, additional structure.
4. **`EvidencePackage.evidence` should also resolve `contradicting_evidence_ids`** (T.1 item 11) — recommended as a small, non-blocking D9 addendum (or D10.2 prerequisite step) so the package is self-sufficient per TAD §42.

None of these four require an ADR (no genuine cross-document architectural conflict); all are calibration-point or interpretation-level decisions in the same class already resolved repeatedly throughout D1-D9, requiring only the user's confirmation before D10 code encodes them.

### T.8 Completeness-Metric Dependency Check (explicit, per directive instruction)

Re-confirmed: TAD §33's `LOW ≥50%/MEDIUM ≥75%/HIGH ≥90%` denominator remains undefined in the current `docs/TAD.md` text (unchanged since D8/D9's own checks). **D10 does not depend on this metric**: TAD §46's Verification pipeline's "coverage verification" step consumes `codex.coverage`'s already-implemented classifications (`CapabilityCoverage`, `NegativeQueryCoverage`, `is_exhaustive_coverage()`) exactly as D9 already does — D10 only needs to know whether the *requested* `CompletenessLevel` was structurally satisfiable (an enum-level, already-implemented check), never to compute an achieved percentage against an undefined denominator. `PARTIALLY_VERIFIED`/`QUALIFIED` (TAD §50) are qualitative verification states, not percentage-driven ones. **Not invented, not silently resolved — confirmed non-blocking for D10, same disposition as D8 and D9.**

### T.9 STOP/GO Decision

No HLRD/TAD contradiction found (T.4). No missing architectural decision materially blocks D10 (T.7's four items are calibration points/interpretations, each with a recommended, non-inventive resolution awaiting confirmation, not a STOP-worthy gap). The one PARTIAL closure item (T.1 #11) is non-blocking with a clear recommended fix. The completeness-metric dependency is confirmed non-blocking (T.8).

**D10 IMPLEMENTATION READY — AWAITING EXPLICIT APPROVAL.**

## U. D10 Implementation — 2026-08-31

Approved per explicit user directive: "APPROVED — PROCEED WITH D10 IMPLEMENTATION," which also locks four binding decisions (T.7's open items) before code: contradiction thresholds `>0.60`/`<0.40` (documented calibration constants, swappable); one shared re-synthesis counter across both trigger types; `ResponseContract` = TAD §44's own schema, no competing structure; `EvidencePackage.evidence` must resolve both `supporting_evidence_ids` and `contradicting_evidence_ids`.

### U.0 D9 addendum (T.1 item 11 fix)

`codex.planner.retrieval.collect_evidence()` now resolves `(*rel.supporting_evidence_ids, *rel.contradicting_evidence_ids)` instead of only the former — `EvidencePackage.evidence` is now the verifier's authoritative, self-sufficient evidence boundary per TAD §42, with no need to reach around the package into `EvidenceStore`. 5 new regression tests in `tests/test_planner_retrieval.py` prove: supporting evidence included, contradicting evidence included, independence-group provenance preserved unmodified, deduplication when an id appears in both roles, and an end-to-end `execute_query()` proof using a hand-crafted `DISPUTED` relationship. 100% coverage maintained on `codex.planner` (527 tests project-wide at this point), clean ruff/mypy.

### U.1-U.9 Implementation summary (D10.1-D10.9)

| Unit | Module(s) | TAD/HLRD source | Summary |
|---|---|---|---|
| D10.1 | `codex.llm.gateway` | TAD §43 | `LLMGateway` Protocol, `LLMRequest`/`LLMGenerationResult`, `GenerationStatus` (honest OK/MALFORMED_OUTPUT/TIMEOUT/BUDGET_EXCEEDED). No mandatory real LLM dependency; `FakeLLMGateway` is the deterministic test double. |
| D10.2 | `codex.llm.schema` | TAD §44-45 | `StructuredAnswer{explanation, claims[]}`, `Claim{subject, predicate, object, claim_type}`. `predicate` accepts `RelationshipType` or `DERIVED_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` (a real gap found and fixed before D10.3: TAD §45's own "DERIVED: A REACHES C" example was otherwise unrepresentable). |
| D10.3 | `codex.verification.entailment` | TAD §47 | `entail_claim()`: direct edge matching (also covers "set membership"/"type hierarchy" — no separate mechanism named by TAD) and bounded path existence (the three derived types, BFS over `EvidencePackage.relationships` only). `SUPPORTED`/`UNRESOLVED` only — contradiction is a separate stage. |
| D10.4 | `codex.verification.confidence`, `.engine` | TAD §46-48 | TAD §48's exact six-factor V formula/weights; D10 Decision 1's thresholds (`>0.60`/`<0.40`/`0.40-0.60` intermediate, `V=min(V,0.50)` for significant only). `provider_authority`/`freshness` reuse existing D2/reconciliation patterns; one new, clearly-scoped calibration point (`CapabilityCoverage` → `[0,1]` for the `coverage` factor). |
| D10.5 | `codex.verification.contradiction` | TAD §49 | `handle_contradictions()`: partitions into retained/removed via D10.4's `is_significantly_contradicted`, generates deterministic REMOVE-only feedback text. |
| D10.6 | `codex.verification.state` | TAD §50 | Canonical `VerificationStatus` (6 values); `classify_claim`/`classify_answer`; `to_hlrd_label`/`to_routing_bucket` reproduce TAD §50's own two tables verbatim. Explicit `EvidenceStatus` vs `VerificationStatus` naming-collision discipline, with a regression test proving the `==` gotcha. |
| D10.7 | `codex.verification.resynthesis` | TAD §44, §49; D10 Decision 2 | `run_verification_loop()`: ONE shared re-synthesis budget across both malformed-output and contradiction triggers (`MAX_ATTEMPTS=2`). Both of the directive's own worked examples verified as named tests. |
| D10.8 | `codex.verification.answer` | HLRD §43, TAD §50 | `build_final_answer()`: HLRD's four-way policy via TAD §50's routing bucket, plus one explicit no-evidence-no-assertion override. Negative-query safety consumes D9's `negative_query_result` unchanged. |
| D10.9 | `tests/test_verification_security.py`, `tests/test_planner_boundaries.py` | directive D10.9 | 21 tests covering every named invariant; AST-based import-boundary checks extended to confirm `codex.planner` still forbids `codex.llm`/`codex.verification` now that they actually exist. |

### V. D10.10-D10.11 — Integration + Final Cross-DTD Audit — 2026-08-31

**D10.10**: `tests/test_d1_d10_integration.py`, 13 tests (scenarios A-M), the real D1-D9 pipeline throughout (nothing mocked except the LLM boundary) — see the commit message for the full scenario list. One real bug found and fixed while writing scenario G: `DeterministicFakeAdapter`'s default `predicate` is `CO_CHANGED_WITH`, not `CALLS` — a test-construction mistake (not a production defect), fixed before committing.

**D10.11 — final validation**: fresh venv, `pytest -q --cov=codex` → **651 tests passed**, 100% on every one of the 8 new D10 modules (`codex.llm.{gateway,schema}`, `codex.verification.{entailment,confidence,engine,contradiction,state,resynthesis,answer}` — 7 verification modules + 2 llm modules), 99% project-wide (pre-existing, unrelated gaps in `evidence/store.py`/`graph/memory_store.py`/`repository/manager.py`, untouched by D10). `ruff check src tests` clean. `mypy src` clean, 65 source files.

**Final cross-DTD contradiction re-check**: repeated the same 15-category sweep as §T.4 against the now-complete implementation (not just the pre-implementation plan) — no new contradiction found; the two previously-open items (T.4 #2 naming collision, #9/#11 re-synthesis counter) are now **closed by implementation**: the naming collision is guarded by a passing regression test (`test_verification_status_is_not_evidence_status`); the re-synthesis counter question is resolved by D10 Decision 2 and proven by both of the directive's own worked examples passing as named tests.

**Explicit verification of the 14 "did not accidentally" checks (directive D10.11), each checked against actual code, not assumed:**

1. **Move retrieval into the LLM** — false. `codex.llm`/`codex.verification` never import `codex.planner`/`codex.graph`/`codex.registry` (AST-verified, D10.9); `LLMRequest` carries only an already-assembled `EvidencePackage`.
2. **Move truth determination into the LLM** — false. `entailment.py`/`confidence.py`/`engine.py`/`contradiction.py`/`state.py` never import `LLMGateway`; `classify_claim` never reads `claim.claim_type` (proven: `test_llm_self_labeling_fact_never_forces_verified_status`).
3. **Allow the LLM to invent evidence** — false. `Claim` carries none of `Evidence`'s identity/provenance fields (proven: `test_claim_type_has_no_evidence_shaped_fields`).
4. **Allow the LLM to bypass EvidencePackage** — false. Every verification function's sole evidence source is the `package` argument it is handed.
5. **Allow the verifier to retrieve arbitrary new evidence** — false. No verification module imports `EvidenceStore`/`GraphReader`/`CapabilityRegistry`; `entail_claim`/`compute_factors` only ever read fields already on the `EvidencePackage`/`EntailmentResult` objects passed in.
6. **Merge EvidenceStatus with VerificationStatus** — false. Distinct enum types (proven, including the documented `==`-via-`StrEnum` gotcha: `test_verification_status_is_not_evidence_status`).
7. **Create a second verification taxonomy** — false. `VerificationStatus` (TAD §50, 6 values) is the only verification taxonomy. `EntailmentStatus` (`SUPPORTED`/`UNRESOLVED`) is a distinct, lower-level *intermediate* signal — never presented externally, never substituted for a `VerificationStatus` value, matching the same "different levels, not competing" pattern as `EvidenceStatus`/`CapabilityCoverage`/`NegativeQueryCoverage` already established across this project.
8. **Create a second contradiction formula** — false. `contradiction_score` is read exclusively from `CanonicalRelationship.contradiction_score` (TAD §38, computed once by the already-implemented Reconciliation Engine); D10 Decision 1's thresholds are classification *bands* on that existing score, not a new formula (verified via direct grep — every `contradiction_score` reference in `codex.verification` reads the field, none recomputes it).
9. **Create a second confidence model** — false. TAD §48's V formula is the only confidence model D10 introduces; D9's separate ranking `score()` (TAD §37, entity/edge relevance) was never touched during D10 (confirmed: `git log` shows no D10 commit modifies `codex/planner/ranking.py`).
10. **Introduce embeddings** — false (grep-verified, no embedding library imported anywhere in `codex.llm`/`codex.verification`).
11. **Introduce fuzzy semantic reasoning** — false. `entail_claim` is exact-match/BFS-only (D10.3, re-verified).
12. **Create unlimited re-synthesis** — false. `MAX_ATTEMPTS=2` is a hard module-level constant; every re-synthesis test (unit and integration) proves `attempts <= 2`.
13. **Weaken negative-query completeness** — false. `git log -- src/codex/coverage/` shows the last commit touching that module predates all of D10 (`9db28ff`, from the gap-closure phase) — `codex.coverage` is untouched by D10; D10.8 consumes `NegativeQueryCoverage` exactly as D9 produced it.
14. **Break graph-version reproducibility** — false. `EvidencePackage.graph_version` is the unmodified `GraphVersion` D9 already locked; D10.10 scenario L explicitly proves identity across `IngestionResult`/`RetrievalPlan`/`EvidencePackage`; no D10 code constructs a new `GraphVersion` or re-fetches a "current" graph.

**No genuine HLRD/TAD contradiction found anywhere in D10.** No ADR required. D10 Decisions 1-4 (approved by the user) are the only calibration/interpretation-level choices, all documented with maximum visibility per this project's entire established discipline.

**D10 — COMPLETE.** Per the directive's explicit closing instruction, D11+ (any further architectural stages) is not started. Stopping after D10.11 for review.

## W. D1-D10 Integration Hardening Pass — 2026-08-31

Per the "focused D1-D10 integration hardening pass" directive, closing the two findings recorded in `docs/architecture-truth-report.md` §12. **D11 was not started.** No HLRD/TAD text was modified, no D1-D10 architecture decision was reopened. Both HLRD/TAD and the truth report were re-read before any change.

### W.1 Finding 1 — symbol-level E2E gap

**Root cause (already diagnosed in the truth report, re-confirmed here):** `tests/fake_ingestion_provider.py`'s `DeterministicFakeAdapter` hardcoded `base_type=BaseEntityType.FILE` at all four entity/relationship construction sites — production code (`GraphReader.find_entities`, `codex.planner.retrieval.resolve_targets`, `codex.planner.retrieval.bounded_traversal`) was already proven correct for symbol-level entities via an out-of-suite manual construction; only the shared test fixture had never exercised it.

**Change made (test infrastructure only, `src/codex/` untouched):**

- `tests/fake_ingestion_provider.py`: `DeterministicFakeAdapter` gained two optional constructor parameters, `base_type: BaseEntityType = BaseEntityType.FILE` (applies to every constructed entity and a relationship pair's subject) and `object_base_type: BaseEntityType | None = None` (applies to a relationship pair's object only, defaulting to `base_type`). Both default to the fixture's pre-existing behavior exactly — **every one of the 651 pre-existing tests using this fixture is unaffected**, confirmed by the full suite passing unmodified.
- `tests/symbol_level_fixtures.py` (new): `build_symbol_level_graph()` registers four `DeterministicFakeAdapter` instances (FILE, FUNCTION, CLASS, METHOD — one real `IngestionPipeline.run()` call, the same multi-provider-coexistence pattern D6 already established for Git+CodeQL/SCIP+CodeQL) producing a mixed graph: 3 FILE entities, 3 FUNCTION entities with 2 real `CALLS` relationships (mirroring this directive's own worked example, "Which tests call `authenticate`?"), 1 CLASS entity, 1 METHOD entity with 1 real `CONTAINS` relationship.
- `tests/test_symbol_level_e2e.py` (new), 3 tests:
  1. `test_mixed_graph_contains_file_function_class_and_method_entities` — proves the fixture itself produces all four base types with real, non-fabricated relationships (not four disjoint islands).
  2. `test_symbol_level_query_end_to_end_via_real_query_understanding` — the full chain **driven by real Tier-0 text detection** (not a hand-built `QueryContract`): `"Which tests call authenticate?"` → `codex.query_understanding.tier0.detect()`/`understand_query()` (D8) → `plan_query()`/`execute_query()` (D9) → `LLMRequest`/`StructuredAnswer` (D10.1-2, LLM boundary faked) → `run_verification_loop()` (D10.3-7) → `build_final_answer()` (D10.8) — reaching `STRONG_ANSWER`/`VERIFIED` with 2 retained claims, 0 removed, no re-synthesis. This is the same query and pipeline shape as the prior audit's one-off, non-committed trace script, now a committed, repeatable regression test.
  3. `test_class_contains_method_relationship_resolves_through_real_retrieval` — a second symbol-level shape (`CLASS --CONTAINS--> METHOD`) via a hand-built `QueryContract` (Tier-0 has no class-containment pattern — an existing, unrelated D8 scope boundary, not changed by this pass), proving retrieval is not accidentally specialized to `FUNCTION`/`CALLS` only.

**One real fixture-naming bug found and fixed during this work, not a production defect:** the first draft named the METHOD entity `"AuthService.authenticate"`, which is a case-insensitive **substring** of the FUNCTION target `"authenticate"` — `GraphReader.find_entities()` is documented, correct substring-match behavior (`src/codex/graph/store.py`'s own docstring: "Deterministic... substring lookup"), so the query for `"authenticate"` was silently also matching the METHOD entity, pulling in an unrelated `CONTAINS` relationship (3 relationships instead of the expected 2). Fixed by renaming the fixture's METHOD entity to `"AuthService.check_credentials"` (no substring overlap) — **no change to `codex.graph`/`codex.planner`**, since the retrieval behavior was already correct and already documented; this was purely a fixture-construction mistake, diagnosed and fixed the same way D10.10 scenario G's predicate mistake was (directive §V).

**Explicitly did not change:** `codex.ontology` (no entity/relationship semantics touched — `BaseEntityType.FUNCTION`/`CLASS`/`METHOD` already existed, unused only because no fixture constructed them), `codex.graph`/`codex.planner` retrieval logic (zero lines changed — `find_entities`/`resolve_targets`/`bounded_traversal` are untouched, already correct), `tests/planner_fixtures.py`'s `build_graph()` (the FILE-only helper used by all pre-existing D9/D10 tests — left exactly as-is, a new, separate fixture module was added instead of modifying the shared one, so no pre-existing test's behavior could possibly change).

### W.2 Finding 2 — provider_authority gap

**Traced TAD §48's `provider_authority` back to D2, per the directive's explicit instruction:** TAD §48 names `provider_authority` as one of six Verification Confidence factors (weight 0.10) but never defines what it measures — confirmed by re-reading TAD §48 directly this pass (no other TAD/HLRD section defines "authority" either). D2's `ProviderScoreProfile.evidence_quality` (TAD §31, ADR-018) is the closest, already-canonical, already-required per-provider trust metric Codex has: normalized `[0.0, 1.0]`, supplied once per provider at `CapabilityRegistry.register()` time, never per query — matching `provider_authority`'s own required shape (`Mapping[str, float]`) exactly, with no invention needed.

**Checked for duplication before wiring it, since TAD §48's own formula already has a separate `evidence_quality` factor (weight 0.20) in the same formula:** re-read `codex.verification.confidence.compute_factors` directly. TAD §48's `evidence_quality` factor is `sum(e.confidence for e in evidence) / len(evidence)` — the average of the **retrieved `Evidence.confidence`** values for one query, a dynamic, per-record quantity computed only from `EvidencePackage.evidence`, never touching registry data. D2's `ProviderScoreProfile.evidence_quality` is static, per-provider registration metadata. The two factors would never read the same underlying numbers — reusing D2's value for `provider_authority` is not a duplication of TAD §48's own `evidence_quality` factor, despite the unfortunate shared field name (the same terminology-overload risk already flagged as **C-4**, now with one more instance recorded).

**Determination: D2 already contains sufficient authoritative data — wired, not invented.**

**Change made (smallest conformant wiring, both changes additive/backward-compatible):**

- `src/codex/registry/registry.py`: `CapabilityRegistry.provider_authority_map() -> dict[str, float]` — a new, read-only accessor returning `{provider_name: profile.evidence_quality}` for every provider with a registered profile. Unlike `rank()`, a provider with no profile is simply absent (not an error) — `provider_authority`'s own historical default (`1.0`, uniform trust) already handles an absent entry safely, and Verification must never fail merely because a provider used only for evidence (not for provider *selection*) lacks a scoring profile. 5 new tests in `tests/test_capability_registry.py`.
- `src/codex/verification/resynthesis.py`: `run_verification_loop()` gained one new optional parameter, `registry: CapabilityRegistry | None = None`. An explicitly-supplied `provider_authority` mapping always wins (every pre-existing caller's exact behavior is unchanged); when `provider_authority` is omitted and `registry` is given, it is derived via `registry.provider_authority_map()`; when both are omitted (the pre-hardening default, still used by every pre-existing test), behavior is byte-identical to before this pass. `engine.py`/`confidence.py` were **not** touched — the mapping is resolved once at the top of `run_verification_loop` and threaded through the existing `provider_authority` parameter unchanged, the smallest change that reaches the real formula. 4 new tests in `tests/test_verification_resynthesis.py`, including one proving the registry-derived value actually lowers `V` relative to the no-registry baseline (not just that the parameter is accepted), one proving explicit-mapping override precedence, and one proving a registry with no profile for the relevant provider safely falls back to the historical default rather than erroring.

**No production call path currently constructs and passes a `registry` argument** — as the truth report noted, no single "answer a query end to end" orchestrator function exists yet anywhere in `src/codex/` (D1-D10 are a set of composable stages, not yet wired behind one entry point; that wiring is implicitly D11+ territory, not started here). `tests/test_symbol_level_e2e.py::test_symbol_level_query_end_to_end_via_real_query_understanding` (§W.1) demonstrates the wiring is used correctly when a caller does have a registry (as any real orchestrator will), passing `registry=registry` into `run_verification_loop()`.

### W.3 Validation

Fresh `.venv-work` run: **663/663 tests passing** (651 pre-existing + 12 new: 3 symbol-level E2E, 5 `provider_authority_map`, 4 `run_verification_loop` registry-wiring), **99% project-wide coverage** (`registry.py`/`resynthesis.py` both still 100% after the additions; the only sub-100% module remains `repository/manager.py`, pre-existing, unrelated), `ruff check src/ tests/` clean, `mypy src/` clean (65 source files). No new pip dependency. No ADR created (no genuine new architectural decision — D2 gap 2 is a documented, non-inventive data-source reuse; the symbol-level fixture gap 1 required no architecture change at all, only a test-double extension).

**No genuine HLRD/TAD contradiction found.** Both HLRD.md and TAD.md, plus `docs/architecture-truth-report.md`, were re-read in full before any change per the directive's explicit instruction.

**W — COMPLETE.** D11 not started. Stopping here for review.

## X. D11 Pre-Implementation Audit — 2026-08-31

Per the explicit "Start D11 Pre-Implementation Audit, but DO NOT implement D11 yet" directive. **Audit-only: no production code, no test code, and no HLRD/TAD text was written or modified in this phase.** Read directly for this audit (not assumed from prior conversation summaries or from this document's own earlier sections): `docs/HLRD.md` in full (all 64 sections, unchanged since the SourceLocation-closure commit — confirmed via `git log`), `docs/TAD.md` in full (all 84 sections, same confirmation), `docs/architecture-conformance-audit.md` §A-W in full (this document, 1034 lines), `docs/architecture-truth-report.md` in full (356 lines), `PROGRESS.md` in full (147 lines). No standalone ADR files exist anywhere in the repository (confirmed: `find . -iname "*adr*"` returns nothing) — "all ADRs" means this document's own §D (ADR Audit) and §I (ADR-018 resolution), both re-read, plus `PROGRESS.md`'s own ADR table (§"Open Items", consistent with §D, no drift found between the two).

### X.1 TAD §33 completeness denominator

**Exactly what is undefined:** TAD §33 states `LOW ≥50%, MEDIUM ≥75%, HIGH ≥90%, EXHAUSTIVE 100%+complete repository coverage`, immediately followed by "these are initial benchmark-calibrated thresholds" — re-read directly, unchanged since every prior audit pass (P.2, R.2, S, truth-report §10). TAD never states the **denominator**: not "% of entities discovered vs. some expected total," not "% of required capabilities that succeeded," not "% of expected evidence records retrieved," not any other quantity. No other HLRD/TAD section supplies it either (HLRD §37-39 discusses coverage/completeness conceptually — "how much of the evidence required by the query has actually been obtained" — but gives no formula, only the qualitative concept `codex.coverage`'s `EXHAUSTIVE` check already fully satisfies without needing a percentage).

**Which future component requires it:** Not D11. `codex.coverage.engine.CompletenessLevel` already propagates LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/EXHAUSTIVE as a requested-level enum through D8→D9→D10 without ever needing to *evaluate* an achieved percentage — confirmed unchanged by this pass (`codex.coverage.engine.py` module docstring re-read, `is_exhaustive_coverage()` is still the only quantitative check). Telemetry (D11's own candidate scope, X.5) only needs to **record** which `CompletenessLevel` was requested and whatever `codex.coverage` already classified (`CapabilityCoverage`/`NegativeQueryCoverage`, both already fully implemented) — it does not need to compute a percentage either. The actual consumer is **TAD §80 Phase 5 (Validation: benchmark repositories, ground truth, metrics, calibration)** — the percentage's denominator can only be meaningfully defined once real ground-truth data exists to decide what it should measure, which is explicitly a later phase than D11.

**Not invented.** No metric was proposed or implemented. **Non-blocking for D11**, re-confirmed a fourth time (P.2, R.2, S, truth-report §10, now this pass) with an identical conclusion each time — this stability across four independent audits is itself evidence the gap is well-understood and correctly parked, not overlooked.

### X.2 TAD §55/§64 CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED

**The missing capability, precisely identified this pass (a sharper finding than any prior audit recorded):** TAD groups two distinct failure modes under one name, and Codex's own code already treats them as two distinct things without saying so out loud:

1. **Retrieval-side version-mismatch detection — already fully built, D9.** `codex.planner.planner.execute_query` (`planner.py:343-348`) raises `GraphVersionMismatchError` — whose message literally reads `"CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED: plan locked graph_version ..."` — the moment a caller executes a `RetrievalPlan` against a `GraphReader` whose `version.version_id` no longer matches the plan's locked version. Tested (`tests/test_planner_graph_version.py:125`). This needs no new detection logic and no ADR.
2. **Ingestion-side concurrent-*writer* conflict detection — genuinely not built, and correctly not built yet.** `codex.ingestion.pipeline.py`'s own module docstring (lines 102-109, re-read this pass) explicitly states: *"Real concurrent-writer detection (TAD §64's `GRAPH_VERSION_CONFLICT`/`CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED`) is not implemented: this pipeline is synchronous/single-threaded... real concurrent-writer detection needs a persistent storage layer with its own transaction semantics (ADR-002 territory), out of D4's scope."* This is a genuinely different capability (detecting two `IngestionPipeline.run()` calls racing to publish) than #1 (detecting a stale reader), correctly deferred to real storage technology (ADR-002), not a D11 concern.

**A third, previously-uncatalogued finding surfaced by this pass:** `codex.planner.models.PlanTelemetry` (`models.py:59-66`) already has a `concurrent_update_detected: bool = False` field — but grep confirms **no code path anywhere ever sets it to `True`**. `execute_query`'s mismatch handling takes an entirely different control-flow path (raising `GraphVersionMismatchError`, `planner.py:344`) that never touches a `RetrievalPlan`/`PlanTelemetry` instance at all — the exception is raised and propagates up, bypassing the very field that was seemingly built to carry this signal. This field is currently **dead** (always `False`, structurally unreachable as `True`), not a defect in D9 (D9's own actual mechanism, the exception, is correct and tested) but a genuine schema/design loose end that D11 will need to reconcile — flagged here, not resolved, since deciding *how* (catch the exception at a call site above `execute_query` and populate a Telemetry record from it? repurpose/remove the dead field? have `execute_query` itself change its signature to return a flagged result instead of raising, which would reopen D9's already-approved contract?) is exactly the kind of implementation-shape decision this audit must not make unilaterally.

**Determination: D11 depends on capability #1 only, which is already satisfied.** D11's Telemetry Store needs to be able to **record** a `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` event; the detection itself (`GraphVersionMismatchError`) already exists and needs no new code. D11 does **not** depend on capability #2 (ingestion-side writer conflict) — that remains correctly deferred to ADR-002/productionization, and D11 must not attempt to build it. The dead `PlanTelemetry.concurrent_update_detected` field is a **required human decision** before D11 implementation touches this specific event (see X.6/X.8), not a blocker for D11's existence.

### X.3 D7 decision

**Kept deferred.** Re-checked the exact condition the directive sets ("unless a deterministic, independently implementable source-graph option is now justified"): the provider-capability matrix is unchanged since the D7 STOP and its two re-audits (§K, §O Phase 3, truth-report §9, re-confirmed a fourth time this pass by re-reading each concrete adapter's `supported_capabilities` — `GitAdapter={HISTORY,CO_CHANGE}`, `SCIPAdapter={SYMBOL_DEFINITION,SYMBOL_REFERENCE,IMPLEMENTATION,TYPE_RELATIONSHIP}`, `CodeQLAdapter={DATA_FLOW}`, zero overlap, zero new capability). No new candidate provider was researched or proposed in this pass (none was in scope — the directive's own instruction is "do not implement D7"). **D7 remains DEFERRED — ARCHITECTURAL DECISION REQUIRED, ADR-006 still open.** Nothing about D11 (Telemetry) creates pressure to reopen it — Telemetry observes whichever providers happen to be registered; it has no opinion on which providers exist.

### X.4 TAD §84 open ADRs — prerequisite classification

Re-read `docs/TAD.md` §84's status table and §77's 17 candidates, and this document's own §D, directly. Classified against the reconstructed D11 scope (X.5):

| ADR | Relation to D11 | Classification |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-001 (Graph Storage) | None — D11 doesn't touch the graph store | Later productionization |
| ADR-002 (Evidence Storage) | Named directly by the ingestion-pipeline docstring (X.2) as the prerequisite for **ingestion-side** concurrent-writer detection, which is explicitly **not** part of D11's scope | Later productionization, not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-003 (Artifact Storage) | Related only if Artifact Store (TAD component #17) is bundled into D11 — see X.5's explicit scoping question; Telemetry Store itself needs no artifact storage | Not a D11 prerequisite for the Telemetry-only scope; would become one only if Artifact Store is bundled in, and even then only for the *technology* choice, not the interface/schema |
| ADR-004/005 (SCIP/CodeQL) | Already resolved, unrelated to D11 | N/A |
| ADR-006 (Sourcegraph/RepoGraph) | Unrelated — D11 doesn't depend on provider count (X.3) | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-007/008 (SLM/LLM selection) | Unrelated — D11 observes LLM call counts/tokens as already-typed data (`LLMGenerationResult`), never needs to know *which* model | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-009 (Embedding strategy) | Unrelated | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-010 (Search/Ranking engine) | Unrelated (infra-only remainder, formula already closed) | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-011 (Cache Technology) | Adjacent but distinct: TAD §54-55's Query-Level Cache (`codex.planner.cache`, already implemented, in-memory) is a different logical store than Telemetry Store (TAD §53 lists them as separate boxes); D11 may *observe* cache hits (TAD §65's own field list names "cache hits") but does not need cache *storage technology* decided to do so | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-012/013 | Closed/superseded, unrelated | N/A |
| ADR-014 (Runtime Adapter) | Unrelated | Not a D11 prerequisite |
| ADR-015 (API Protocol) | Relevant only to *soliciting* user feedback over a real API (HLRD §46/TAD §60) — no API/UI layer exists anywhere in Codex yet (correctly deferred, TAD §69/HLRD §54); D11 can define the feedback *record shape* and accept it as a typed input without needing a live API to collect it from | Not a D11 prerequisite; blocks only the (out-of-scope) live feedback-collection surface |
| ADR-016 (Auth) | Unrelated to a first in-memory Telemetry Store; becomes relevant only once Telemetry is exposed over a real API/multi-tenant deployment | Later productionization |
| ADR-017 (Deployment) | Unrelated | Later productionization |

**No explicit "Telemetry Storage Technology" ADR is named anywhere in TAD §77's 17 candidates** — a genuine omission in TAD's own ADR list (TAD §53's Storage Architecture diagram lists "Telemetry Store" as one of five logical storage boxes, but the corresponding ADR was never enumerated alongside ADR-001/002/003/011 for the other four). This is not a contradiction (nothing asserts the opposite), just an incompleteness in TAD's own bookkeeping — flagged here for the record, not silently patched into TAD's text (per the explicit "no document rewriting" rule) and not blocking, since **every store Codex has built so far (`InMemoryGraphStore`, `InMemoryEvidenceStore`, `PlanCache`) was built as an in-memory reference implementation behind a Protocol well before its own storage-technology ADR was resolved** — this is V1's established, repeated pattern, not a new one D11 would be inventing.

**Conclusion: zero of the 17 ADR candidates are blocking prerequisites for D11 as scoped in X.5.** All remain correctly deferred to later productionization (TAD §80 Phase 6), exactly as TAD §84 itself frames them ("🟡 ADR... where we want to be before writing the actual implementation" of *technology* — not of the interface).

### X.5 D11 boundary reconstruction

**Component identified:** TAD §6's component list (re-read directly, all 18 named) has exactly three components with no code anywhere in `src/codex/`: **#16 Telemetry Store**, **#17 Artifact Store**, **#18 Offline Calibration Pipeline**. TAD §59's own pipeline diagram (*"Production Telemetry → Training Dataset → Offline Evaluation → Calibration → Shadow → Canary → Production"*) makes #18 explicitly downstream of #16 — Offline Calibration needs telemetry data to exist first, and Codex has never run against a real repository/real query traffic, so no such data exists yet regardless. **#18 is therefore out of D11's reach by construction**, not by choice.

That leaves #16 (Telemetry Store) and #17 (Artifact Store) as the two candidates. They are related (TAD §53 lists both as "Storage Architecture" siblings, both currently `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`) but serve different purposes: #16 is a cross-cutting *observability sink* (TAD §75: *"Telemetry → all runtime components"* — the one component TAD explicitly authorizes to depend on everything else); #17 is a narrow *artifact-reference resolution service* (TAD §52: resolving `Evidence.raw_reference`/`ExtractionResult.raw_reference` URIs, whose scheme validation is already done, D1, but whose actual resolution service does not exist). **This audit reconstructs D11's primary scope as #16 (Telemetry Store)**, per TAD §75's dependency rule making it the more architecturally central "next" component, and flags #17 as a **closely related, smaller, separable component** — whether to fold it into D11 or treat it as a distinct D12 is an open scoping question for the human reviewer (X.8), not decided here.

**Inputs (TAD §65/HLRD §52, cross-checked, X.6):** already-typed data every existing D1-D10 module already produces — `query_id`/`query_identity` (`codex.planner.cache.compute_query_identity`, exists), `repository_id`+`graph_version` (`GraphVersion`, exists), `QueryContract` (D8, exists), `RetrievalPlan` including its own `PlanTelemetry` (D9, exists — see X.2's dead-field finding), candidate/entity/relationship counts (`EvidencePackage`, exists), LLM call count/tokens (`LLMGenerationResult`, exists — token counts specifically are not yet a populated field anywhere, see X.6), `ClaimVerification`/`ResynthesisResult`/`FinalAnswer` (D10, exists), `ProviderRunOutcome` (D4, exists, already carries failure/skip/partial detail), user feedback (HLRD §46/TAD §60 — no producer exists anywhere yet, since no API/UI layer exists; D11 can define the typed shape without a live source).

**Outputs:** a `TelemetryRecord` (or a small family of typed records, one per lifecycle stage) persisted into a new `TelemetryStore` Protocol + in-memory implementation, following the exact `EvidenceStore`/`GraphStore`/`PlanCache` pattern already established three times over. Read-only for every consumer — nothing in D1-D10 reads from it (TAD §59: production doesn't perform unrestricted online learning; feedback/telemetry data only flows into the *offline*, not-yet-built Calibration Pipeline, #18).

**Dependencies:** reads (never mutates) the already-public types of `codex.ontology`, `codex.graph`, `codex.query_understanding`, `codex.planner`, `codex.llm`, `codex.verification`, `codex.provider`/`codex.ingestion` (for `ProviderRunOutcome`) — the one component TAD §75 explicitly authorizes to depend on the entire rest of the system. No existing module would import the new `codex.telemetry` package (verified as a design constraint, not yet as code — see X.7).

**Invariants (derived from HLRD/TAD text, not invented):**
- Never mutates the canonical graph/evidence store (TAD §62, invariants #6-8, extended by the same reasoning already applied to Verification/LLM).
- Must not become *"an uncontrolled shadow copy of repository source code"* (HLRD §53, verbatim) — captures the structured/summary fields TAD §65/HLRD §52 actually name, never raw file content or full `EvidencePackage` payloads.
- Feedback is *"a learning signal, not automatically ground truth"* (HLRD §46, verbatim) — stored as reported, never validated or promoted to fact by Telemetry itself.
- *"Feedback does not directly modify the canonical graph"* (TAD §60) / *"Learned intelligence SHALL NOT directly modify canonical repository truth"* (HLRD INV-008) — Telemetry has no write path back into `codex.graph`/`codex.evidence`, structurally, the same enforcement pattern D10.9's boundary tests already established for the LLM.
- Retention is configurable (HLRD §53) — the *policy* is a later concern; a first in-memory implementation with no retention enforcement is not a violation of this invariant, matching how `InMemoryGraphStore`/`InMemoryEvidenceStore` also don't yet enforce HLRD §53's graph-version retention policy either.

**Explicit non-goals for D11:**
- Offline Calibration Pipeline (#18) — downstream, needs real data D11 will only just start producing.
- Real storage technology selection (no ADR exists or is needed to be resolved first, X.4) — an in-memory reference implementation only, exactly like every prior store.
- A live API/UI surface to actually collect user feedback — HLRD §54/TAD §69 explicitly defer external API protocol to a later technical-design deliverable; D11 can accept a typed feedback record as a parameter without inventing a collection endpoint.
- Using telemetry data to alter runtime behavior (ranking weights, routing thresholds, provider selection, etc.) at query time — explicitly forbidden by TAD §59 ("production runtime does not perform unrestricted online learning"); any such use is Offline Calibration's job, and only after Shadow/Canary validation per TAD §59's own lifecycle diagram.
- Ingestion-side concurrent-writer detection (X.2 capability #2) — correctly deferred to ADR-002.
- Reopening or modifying `RetrievalPlan.telemetry`/`PlanTelemetry`'s existing schema, `execute_query`'s exception-raising contract, or any other already-approved D9/D10 decision (X.2's dead-field finding is reported as a decision *point*, not pre-resolved here).

### X.6 Cross-document audit affecting D11

Compared HLRD §52's telemetry field list against TAD §65's directly, field by field (both re-read in full this pass):

| HLRD §52 field | TAD §65 field | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| Query ID | `query_id` | Match |
| Repository / Revision | `repository_id` / `graph_version` | Match (TAD's `graph_version` subsumes revision via its composite key, TAD §19 — not a conflict, a more precise successor field) |
| Query intent / Complexity | `query_contract` | Match (TAD's field is the whole typed object, HLRD names two of its members individually — narrower-vs-broader framing, same pattern as every prior D8/D9 "Granularity note" in this document, not a contradiction) |
| Query plan | `retrieval_plan` | Match (same concept, TAD's name matches the actual `RetrievalPlan` type) |
| Providers used | `selected_providers` | Match |
| Provider failures | `provider_failures` | Match |
| Retrieval operations | *(not separately named)* | HLRD-only; `candidate counts`/`retrieval_plan` partially cover it — genuinely narrower in TAD, not contradicted |
| Candidate counts | `candidate counts` | Match |
| Ranking signals | *(not named)* | **HLRD-only field TAD's §65 list omits**, even though TAD §36-37 fully specifies named ranking signals and `codex.planner.ranking` already computes them — a real, if minor, granularity gap worth flagging for X.8, not a contradiction (TAD's list is a narrower V1 baseline, not an exclusion) |
| Selected subgraph / Coverage | *(not named)* | Same pattern — `codex.coverage`'s types already exist and could populate this, TAD's §65 list just doesn't name it explicitly |
| Model calls / Token usage | `LLM calls` / `LLM tokens` | Match |
| Latency | `latency` | Match |
| Verification status | `verification result` | Match |
| Answer outcome | *(not named)* | HLRD-only; D10.8's `AnswerDecision` is a distinct field from `VerificationStatus` that TAD §65 doesn't separately name — same minor granularity gap |
| User feedback | `user feedback` | Match |
| *(not in HLRD)* | `MSS size` | TAD-only, more V1-specific than HLRD's higher-level list — not a conflict |
| *(not in HLRD)* | `unsupported claims` / `contradictions` | TAD-only, reflects D10's richer verification taxonomy — not a conflict |
| *(not in HLRD)* | `cache hits` | TAD-only, reflects the now-implemented D9 cache — not a conflict |

**Conclusion: no genuine HLRD↔TAD contradiction regarding Telemetry's field list.** Every difference is the same "TAD narrows HLRD's broader/aspirational list to a concrete V1 set" pattern this document has already documented three times (D8's intent-vocabulary Granularity note, D9's ranking-signals Granularity note, and now this one) — never a case where the two documents assert incompatible things. The handful of HLRD-only fields (ranking signals, selected subgraph/coverage, answer outcome) are readily available from already-implemented types with zero invention required, but per this project's own established discipline (P.1 explicitly excluded `reasoning_requirement` from `QueryContract` for exactly this reason — "would invent a field TAD's own struct omits") **whether to include them is a documented-interpretation decision for D11's actual implementation, not resolved here.**

Checked D1-D10 implementation against both documents for anything already telemetry-shaped that could conflict: `PlanTelemetry` (D9) and `PlanCache`'s `cache_hit` field are the only telemetry-*shaped* data structures that exist, and neither is a persistence layer — both are ephemeral, per-call, in-memory fields on already-returned objects (`RetrievalPlan.telemetry`), fully consistent with feeding into, not competing with, a future `TelemetryStore`. No conflicting or duplicate telemetry concept found anywhere in the codebase.

### X.7 Dependency-direction confirmation

Re-derived the same import-graph extraction method as the truth report's §7 (`grep -rhoE "^from codex\.[a-z_]+" src/codex/<pkg>/*.py`, all 13 existing top-level packages) — unchanged since that audit (no source file has been added to or removed from any package's import list by the hardening pass, confirmed by re-running the exact same command this pass and diffing against the truth report's recorded graph: identical). The existing chain remains a strict DAG with `verification` as its current terminal node (query_understanding → planner → llm → verification, TAD §1's authoritative pipeline order, HLRD §4's principle diagram).

A new `codex.telemetry` package, as scoped in X.5, would sit as a **strict successor** of every existing node — importing from `ontology`, `graph`, `query_understanding`, `planner`, `llm`, `verification`, `provider`/`ingestion` (for `ProviderRunOutcome`) to type its capture functions, while **zero existing package would import `codex.telemetry` back** (nothing in the D1-D10 chain has any reason to read telemetry data — TAD §59's "no unrestricted online learning" invariant forbids exactly that feedback loop at runtime). This preserves the DAG property by construction — telemetry can only be *appended* to the dependency graph, never *inserted into* the existing Query Understanding → Planner/Retrieval → EvidencePackage → LLM → Verification → Final Answer chain, because nothing in that chain would ever need to call into it to produce its own output. **Confirmed: D11 can be added without bypassing or reordering any existing stage.**

### X.8 Required human decisions (none pre-resolved by this audit)

1. **Scope: Telemetry Store alone, or Telemetry Store + Artifact Store together as D11?** (X.5) Both are small, related, currently-unimplemented storage siblings; TAD's own component numbering (#16 vs #17) and differing purposes support treating them separately, but nothing forbids bundling them either.
2. **How to bridge `GraphVersionMismatchError` into a `CONCURRENT_UPDATE_DETECTED` telemetry record, and what to do with the currently-dead `PlanTelemetry.concurrent_update_detected` field** (X.2) — catch-at-call-site, repurpose the field, or another shape; this touches D9's existing (approved, tested) contract at the margin and should not be decided silently.
3. **Whether to include the HLRD-only telemetry fields TAD §65 doesn't name** (ranking signals, selected subgraph/coverage, answer outcome — X.6) in D11's actual record schema, consistent with this project's "don't invent a field neither document's authoritative struct lists" discipline, or treat TAD §65's list as the closed baseline.
4. **Whether/when to also implement real feedback *collection*** (requires the still-deferred API/UI layer, ADR-015) versus only defining the feedback record *shape* now and leaving collection for later (X.5's non-goals).

None of these block starting D11's scoping or design — they are exactly the class of "flag with maximum visibility, do not silently resolve" decision points this project has handled the same way at every prior D-phase boundary (ADR-018, the D9 latency-budget analogy, D10 Decisions 1-4).

### X.9 Verdict

**GO WITH CONDITIONS.** No genuine HLRD/TAD contradiction was found (X.6). No blocking architectural decision prevents scoping or beginning D11 (X.4). D7 correctly stays deferred and does not gate D11 (X.3). TAD §33's gap is non-blocking for a fourth consecutive audit (X.1). TAD §55/§64's gap is precisely characterized and shown to be satisfiable within D11's own scope, with one genuinely out-of-scope sub-case (ingestion-side writer conflict) correctly excluded (X.2). D11's boundary — Telemetry Store, TAD component #16, a strict DAG successor of the entire existing pipeline with zero back-edges (X.7) — is reconstructed directly from TAD §6/§59/§65/§75 and HLRD §44-46/§52-53 text, not invented. Four items (X.8) require an explicit human decision before implementation proceeds; none are blocking in the sense of preventing D11 from being scoped, only in the sense of needing a decision this audit is not authorized to make. **D11 was not implemented.** No production code, test code, or HLRD/TAD text was written or modified in this phase. Stopping here for review.
