## II. D9 Target-Resolution Refinement — 2026-09-01

Per the "D9 Target-Resolution Refinement — Implement Only the Benchmark Finding" directive, fixing the specific target-resolution failure a 24-query real-repository benchmark identified (benchmark findings #12/#13/#22/#24 — "What does veyra/codex depend on?", "What calls extract?"/"run?"). D9 refinement only; D1-D8/D10-D13/providers/ontology untouched; no new dependency; no LLM/SLM/embedding/fuzzy resolution.

### II.1 Root cause

`GraphReader.find_entities(name=..., qualified_name=...)` (`InMemoryGraphStore`, unchanged) does case-insensitive **substring** containment, per HLRD §34. `resolve_targets` (`codex.planner.retrieval`) unions both axes' results with equal weight. `plan_query` then rejects the whole plan as `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` — before any traversal — whenever `len(target_entities) > max_nodes` (TAD §41).

Reproduced directly against the real repositories: a target string that is also a repository's own name substring-matches **597** entities by `qualified_name` in `veyra` (every path under `src/veyra/...`) and **1,295** in `codex`, against only **2** by `name` in each — confirming the explosion is almost entirely a `qualified_name`-axis artifact of a short string coinciding with a directory name. A short, common *symbol* name ("extract", "run") instead produces **near-identical** counts on both axes (98/98, 92/92 for "extract"/"run" in `codex`; 13/13 for "classify" in `veyra`) — no such asymmetry, because in that case the over-match is genuine: many distinct real entities really do have (or substring-contain) that name. `DEFAULT_TOKEN_BUDGET = 4000` gives `max_nodes = min(100, 4000 // 50) = 80`, so both pathologies (repo name and common symbol name) legitimately exceed it and hit the same `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` branch, with zero traversal ever attempted, zero evidence ever considered.

### II.2 Exact fix

Two independent, additive pieces, both in `codex.planner` (D9's own package):

1. **`_resolve_one_target` (`codex.planner.retrieval`, new function `resolve_targets` now delegates to per target)**: `find_entities(qualified_name=target)`'s own result set is narrowed to only its *exact* (case-insensitive full-string-equal) matches when at least one exists; the full substring set is kept only when no exact match exists. The `find_entities(name=target)` axis is **never** narrowed — always the full substring set, exactly as before. This asymmetry is deliberate and load-bearing, not stylistic: an earlier version narrowed both axes uniformly and was reverted after it reproduced a real regression (§II.5) — SCIP-derived entities carry a trailing `().`/`ClassName#` decoration (`codex.provider.scip.mapping`) and therefore *never* produce a bare exact `name` match for a callable, so narrowing the `name` axis silently dropped real SCIP evidence (including the real test-caller edges for "Which tests call classify?"). The `qualified_name` axis has no equivalent cost: real symbol-name pathologies show no qualified-name/name asymmetry to begin with (confirmed in II.1), so narrowing that axis alone fixes the repository-name pathology with nothing to lose.

2. **`plan_query`'s target-entity budget check (`codex.planner.planner`)**: when (after step 1) `len(target_entities) > max_nodes`, the previous unconditional `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` now applies **only** when `is_exhaustive` or `max_nodes == 0`. Otherwise, `target_entities` is deterministically truncated to the first `max_nodes` entities in `resolve_targets`'s own established canonical-id sort order, a new pruning step (`"reduce target-entity set to budget (N -> M)"`) is appended to the *existing* `pruning_steps`/`BudgetTrace` record (TAD §32's own "original estimate, pruned estimate, whether pruning occurred, reason" — no new field, no schema change), and planning continues normally (`bounded_traversal`, ranking, etc. — all unmodified) from the truncated seed set. `PlanStatus` gains no new value; the plan settles into the existing `OK`/`PRUNED` outcome exactly as any other pruning step would.

No change to `GraphReader`/`InMemoryGraphStore.find_entities`, `bounded_traversal`, `rank_entities`, `collect_evidence`, `PlanStatus`, `RetrievalPlan`, `BudgetTrace`'s schema, or any D1-D8/D10-D13 module.

### II.3 Why it preserves existing invariants

- **HLRD §34 (deterministic substring lookup)**: `find_entities` itself is untouched; both axes still do plain substring matching. The refinement only changes which of `find_entities`'s own already-deterministic results `resolve_targets` keeps.
- **EXHAUSTIVE (TAD §32, "cannot be pruned below required coverage")**: Tier 2 (truncation) is unconditionally skipped for `is_exhaustive`, byte-for-byte preserving today's `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED` behavior on that path (verified: `test_exhaustive_query_still_plan_unsupported_when_target_set_exceeds_budget`). Tier 1 (exact-match narrowing) is not EXHAUSTIVE-conditional, but narrowing to a genuine exact identity match discards nothing an EXHAUSTIVE query needed — the excluded entities were never anything but a substring coincidence, not real coverage (`test_exhaustive_query_also_benefits_from_clean_exact_match_narrowing`).
- **Negative-query safety**: both tiers are no-ops on an already-empty target set (a genuinely nonexistent symbol still resolves to `[]`, still flags `negative_query_candidate=True`) — verified directly.
- **Canonical-ID semantics**: untouched; nothing here computes, reinterprets, or converges canonical IDs — only *which already-computed* entities become traversal seeds.
- **Ranking fidelity / MSS / token bounds**: `rank_entities`, `bounded_traversal`'s own edge/node ceilings, and MSS expansion are all downstream of the (now possibly smaller) seed set and are entirely unmodified — a smaller, more precise seed set changes *what* gets traversed, never *how*.
- **Determinism**: exact-match narrowing is pure string-equality filtering on `find_entities`'s own already-sorted-and-deduplicated output; truncation uses the pre-existing `sorted(..., key=lambda e: e.canonical_id)` order `resolve_targets` already established — same seed set on every repeated call, verified directly (`test_truncation_is_deterministic_across_repeated_calls`).
- **"Do not silently discard valid candidates merely to improve benchmark scores"**: every discarded qualified-name substring match, when an exact match exists, is discarded because a *stronger*, well-founded signal (this literally is the named entity) already supersedes it — not an arbitrary cut. Truncation is disclosed explicitly via `pruning_steps`/`reason`, never silent.

### II.4 Before/after — all 24 benchmark queries

Re-ran the unmodified `benchmark.py` harness (same real `veyra`/`codex` repository copies, same real SCIP indexes, same 24 queries, same D10 claim variants) before and after this fix.

| # | Query | Repo | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 12 | "What does veyra depend on?" | veyra | `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, 0 entities | **`OK`, 3 entities, 1 `DEPENDS_ON` relationship** (real: `pytest`) |
| 13 | "What does codex depend on?" | codex | `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, 0 entities | **`OK`, 9 entities, 7 `DEPENDS_ON` relationships** (real: `networkx`/`pydantic`/`GitPython`/`pytest`/`pytest-cov`/`ruff`/`mypy`) |
| 22 | "What calls extract?" | codex | `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, 0 entities | **`PRUNED`, 80 entities, 58 relationships**, `CALL_RELATIONSHIP: COMPLETE`, explicit `"reduce target-entity set to budget (98 -> 80)"` |
| 24 | "What calls run?" | codex | `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, 0 entities | **`PRUNED`, 80 entities, 103 relationships**, `CALL_RELATIONSHIP: COMPLETE`, explicit `"reduce target-entity set to budget (92 -> 80)"` |

All other 20 queries (including the 3 D10 claim variants on "What calls classify?" — `STRONG_ANSWER`/`ABSTAIN`/`ABSTAIN`, unchanged) are **byte-identical** to the pre-fix baseline: same `plan_status`, `entity_count`, `relationship_count`, `coverage`, `negative_query_result`, `d10_final_decision`, and `intent`, confirmed by a field-by-field diff of the full `benchmark_results.json` before and after.

### II.5 Regressions found and fixed during development (not present in the final diff)

The first implementation narrowed **both** axes (`name` and `qualified_name`) on exact match. Re-running the benchmark immediately surfaced a real regression: "What calls classify?" / "Which tests call classify?" (veyra) dropped from 23 to 4 entities, losing every real test-caller edge (`test_classify_and_audit_persists_the_result()`, etc.) — because those edges were only reachable through SCIP's own `classify().`-named entity (a `qualified_name`/`name` pair that never equals bare `"classify"`), which the over-broad `name`-axis narrowing silently excluded. Diagnosed precisely (§II.1's per-axis match-count comparison) and fixed by restricting narrowing to the `qualified_name` axis only (§II.2). Re-verified: "Which tests call classify?" is back to 23 entities/21 relationships, byte-identical to the pre-fix baseline. This regression never reached the committed code — it is recorded here because it directly shaped the final, narrower design and the two axis-asymmetry unit tests it added.

### II.6 Tests

13 new tests in `tests/test_planner_target_resolution.py`, 100% coverage on both changed modules:

- **Unit (`_resolve_one_target`/`resolve_targets`, 5 tests)**: repository-name collision narrows to the one exact `qualified_name` match; the `name` axis keeps full substring recall for SCIP-decorated entities (the exact regression from §II.5, now a permanent regression test); ambiguous targets (5 distinct entities sharing one exact name) all return, deterministically sorted; no exact match keeps the full substring set; an exact `name` match alone does not narrow the `qualified_name` axis.
- **Integration, through `plan_query`/`execute_query` (3 tests)**: full reproduction of the repository-name-collision fix (`status=OK`, no truncation even needed, since Tier 1 alone resolves it); full reproduction of the common-short-name-over-budget fix (`status=PRUNED`, explicit pruning step, real evidence retrieved); deterministic truncation across three repeated calls.
- **EXHAUSTIVE preservation (2 tests)**: an EXHAUSTIVE query over an oversized target set (no exact match available) still gets unconditional `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, `pruning_occurred=False`; an EXHAUSTIVE query with a clean exact match still succeeds (Tier 1 applies unconditionally, before the EXHAUSTIVE branch is even reached).
- **Budget edge case (1 test)**: `max_nodes == 0` still gets `PLAN_UNSUPPORTED`, never "truncate to an empty seed set and continue."
- **Negative-query safety (2 tests)**: a genuinely nonexistent symbol still resolves to an empty target set and flags `negative_query_candidate`; a repository-name-shaped target that doesn't exist in the graph at all still resolves to nothing.

### II.7 Validation

Fresh `.venv-work` run: **961/961 tests passing** (948 → 961: +13), **100% coverage** on both changed modules (`codex/planner/retrieval.py`, `codex/planner/planner.py`), 99% project-wide, `ruff check src/ tests/` clean, `mypy src/` clean (79 source files, unchanged count — no new modules). No new pip dependency. No ADR. No HLRD/TAD text modified. Real-repository benchmark re-run confirms §II.4's before/after table exactly, with zero diffs across two independent re-runs after the final fix (full determinism confirmed).

**D1-D8/D10-D13/providers/ontology untouched** — only `codex.planner.retrieval` and `codex.planner.planner` (both already-existing D9 modules) were edited. No new `PlanStatus` value, no new `BudgetTrace`/`RetrievalPlan` field, no embeddings/fuzzy/semantic matching anywhere.

**GO.** All four benchmark failures fixed with a narrow, contract-consistent refinement; zero regressions across the full test suite and the full 24-query benchmark; EXHAUSTIVE/negative-query/determinism/canonical-ID invariants all directly verified. Stopping here for review, per the directive's explicit closing instruction — no further phase started automatically.

## JJ. Cross-Provider Entity Convergence — 2026-09-01

Per the "Implement the Approved Cross-Provider Entity-Convergence Fix" directive, closing HLRD §19's "Codex SHALL resolve equivalent provider entities into canonical entities" for the one live case the D7/D9 research phase (§II's sibling research report) found: `SCIPAdapter` and `AstCallsAdapter` (D7) independently reporting `FUNCTION`/`METHOD`/`CLASS` entities for the same real symbol, under incompatible naming schemes, never converging. Exactly two changes, both in scope of that directive; no new dependency, no schema/ontology change, no D8/D9/D10/D11/D12/D13 modification, no provider-to-provider coupling.

### JJ.1 Change 1 — `AstCallsAdapter` `SourceLocation` correction

`ast_calls_adapter._location()` previously stored Python's own `ast` module line numbers (1-based, inclusive-both-ends) verbatim into `SourceLocation` (0-based, half-open per its own established convention, closed 2026-08-31). Fixed to convert: `start_line = record.lineno - 1`, `end_line = record.end_lineno - 1`; columns need no change (`ast`'s `col_offset`/`end_col_offset` already match `SourceLocation`'s convention). Verified against real `veyra` data: SCIP's own `classify()` definition (`start_line=16`) now matches `AstCallsAdapter`'s corrected `start_line=16` for the identical real line (was `17` before the fix — the exact off-by-one the D7/D9 research report identified as the blocker for any location-based identity key). 3 new regression tests in `tests/test_ast_calls_adapter.py` (0-based single-line location, multi-line definition's `end_line`, columns unchanged) — 52/52 tests in that file passing, 100% coverage maintained.

### JJ.2 Change 2 — symbol-location identity key (`codex.resolution.entity_resolver`)

Extends the *existing* `resolve_entities`/`_merge_pair` mechanism (no new component) with a second, independent identity axis alongside the pre-existing path-normalization key: `(repository_id, repository_revision, base_type, file_path, start_line)` — the approved key exactly, read from `RepositorySymbol.source_location`. Never `name`/`qualified_name` (incompatible schemes between SCIP's descriptor paths and `AstCallsAdapter`'s `<path>::<name>` scheme), never fuzzy/embedding/probabilistic matching, never a provider-specific identifier. An entity with no eligible base type (`FUNCTION`/`METHOD`/`CLASS` only — `EXTERNAL_LIBRARY` has no in-repository position) or no `source_location` is untouched, trusted as-is, exactly the module's pre-existing "only use signals the architecture actually supports" principle. `build_canonical_id` is reused unmodified, fed a synthesized `qualified_name` tagged `codex:symbol-location-identity:<file_path>:<start_line>` (never colliding with a real provider's own qualified_name shape) — the canonical id computation itself is not replaced, only which input string produces it. `MatchReason` gains one additive value, `SYMBOL_LOCATION_IDENTITY`.

**Applied unconditionally** — every eligible entity recomputes to the location-derived id, whether or not a merge partner is present in the batch that happens to be resolving it, exactly mirroring how path normalization already behaves for FILE/DIRECTORY. This was not the first design tried; see JJ.5.

### JJ.3 Why the key is safe (false-merge investigation)

Every candidate false-merge scenario the directive named was constructed and tested, all in `tests/test_entity_resolution.py`:

- **Same type, different line** (`test_symbol_location_same_type_different_line_does_not_converge`): `start_line` differs → no merge.
- **Same bare name, different file** (`test_symbol_location_same_name_different_file_does_not_converge`): two distinct real `execute` methods in different files/lines never merge just because `name` matches — only location does. Confirmed against real data too (JJ.6): `veyra` has three distinct real `execute` methods (`ExecutionBoundary#execute` at `boundary.py:82`, `DockerExecutionBoundary#execute` at `docker_boundary.py:108`, `FakeExecutionBoundary#execute` at `test_boundary_contract.py:30`) — only the one with a genuine SCIP+AST location match (`DockerExecutionBoundary#execute`) converges; the other two stay distinct, proving the key doesn't over-merge on name collision.
- **Different entity type, same location** (`test_symbol_location_different_entity_type_same_location_does_not_converge`): `base_type` is part of the key — a FUNCTION and a METHOD reported at the same file/line never merge.
- **Different revision** (`test_symbol_location_different_revision_does_not_converge`): `repository_revision` is part of the key.
- **Same-file/same-line edge case**: this is exactly the intended positive case (two providers genuinely describing the same real definition span) — not a false-merge risk, since `start_line` is the definition's own line, deterministic per provider, and already verified to agree between SCIP and the fixed `AstCallsAdapter` (JJ.1). No safer discriminator was found or needed: `end_line`/columns were considered and rejected as *less* stable (SCIP's own multi-line-span conventions for a class/function are its own implementation detail, already observed to vary independently of the definition's start), so the key stays exactly as approved — never silently broadened.

No provider imports another's output; `SCIPAdapter` and `AstCallsAdapter` remain fully independent extract/normalize implementations. Convergence happens only inside `resolve_entities`, called only from `IngestionPipeline._commit_entities`, unchanged call site.

### JJ.4 Real SCIP/AST convergence examples

Directly measured (raw `SCIPAdapter`+`AstCallsAdapter` output, before resolution) against real `veyra`: **256 real symbol-level convergences** out of 1,062 raw entities (806 resolved), all tagged `SYMBOL_LOCATION_IDENTITY`, all order-independent, all with distinct, non-colliding source locations. Named examples verified against real source: `classify()` (`classification.py:16`), `classify_and_audit()` (`audit.py:16`), `DockerExecutionBoundary.execute()` (`docker_boundary.py:108`), and (checked-in fixture, `tests/test_symbol_convergence.py`) this repository's own `resolve_entities`/`_merge_pair`/`_normalized_identity_key`/`_recompute_canonical_id`/`_symbol_location_identity_key`/`_recompute_symbol_canonical_id` (`src/codex/resolution/entity_resolver.py`) — 6/6 real `AstCallsAdapter`-detected functions in that file converge with their real, independently `scip-python`-indexed SCIP counterparts.

### JJ.5 Regression found and fixed during development (not present in the final diff)

The first implementation gated the symbol-location key on **cardinality**: only recompute/rename an entity's id when a second raw entity in the *same* `resolve_entities()` call already shared its key, leaving a lone (so-far single-provider) symbol's original provider-computed id untouched — reasoning, at the time, that renaming every singleton unconditionally was an avoidable, unnecessary blast radius.

Validating this against real `veyra` ingestion (all four providers) surfaced a genuine, serious regression: `classify`/`classify().` correctly converged into one entity, but the real `classify_and_audit -> classify` `CALLS` relationship — present and correct before this work — **disappeared** from the materialized graph. Root cause, traced precisely through `IngestionPipeline._run_one_provider`/`_commit_entities`/`_commit_evidence` (`src/codex/ingestion/pipeline.py`): providers commit in alphabetical name order (`ast_calls` before `scip`); `AstCallsAdapter`'s `CALLS` evidence for `classify_and_audit -> classify` is committed *first*, against its own raw (still-singleton, since `scip` hasn't run yet) id; `SCIPAdapter`'s later commit re-resolves the accumulated entity set, *now* finds the merge, and renames both entities' ids — but `_commit_evidence` only ever remaps the evidence the *current* provider just normalized, never revisits an earlier provider's already-committed evidence. `EvidenceStore`/`InMemoryEvidenceStore` (`src/codex/evidence/store.py`) has no update/re-key operation by design (append-only: `add_evidence`/`get_evidence`/`get_evidence_for` only), confirmed by direct inspection before ruling out a fix there. `AstCallsAdapter`'s evidence was left pointing at a now-defunct id and was silently dropped by `_materialize_store`'s pre-existing referential-integrity check — a real, true relationship destroyed by the very feature meant to make Codex more correct, violating this module's own "no evidence is ever discarded" principle and HLRD §19's "shall preserve all provider references rather than deleting them."

Fixed by removing the cardinality gate: the symbol-location key is applied **unconditionally** (JJ.2), exactly mirroring the pre-existing, already-safe FILE/DIRECTORY path-normalization precedent. Because the target id is a pure function of `(repository_id, repository_revision, base_type, file_path, start_line)` alone — never of how many providers have reported it *so far* — a provider's own first-ever commit of a symbol already receives its final, stable id, so that provider's own evidence (committed in the same call) can never later go stale. Re-verified against real `veyra`: the `classify_and_audit -> classify` `CALLS` relationship is present; referential integrity holds (0 dangling relationships) across both `veyra` (810 entities/2,934 relationships) and `codex` (2,274 entities/3,697 relationships) after the fix. Two dedicated regression tests reproduce the exact scenario minimally (`test_first_providers_own_evidence_survives_a_later_convergence`, `test_provider_order_does_not_change_the_evidence_survival_outcome`, `tests/test_symbol_convergence.py`) plus a real-data version (`test_real_codex_source_scip_and_ast_functions_converge`).

One consequence of unconditional application, checked and accepted: a symbol-level entity's canonical id, when computed via location, is no longer guaranteed to equal `build_canonical_id` computed independently from its raw provider `qualified_name` — even for a still-single-provider entity. Grepped `src/` for any other caller depending on this equality: none (`build_canonical_id` is called only by providers at extraction time and by `entity_resolver` itself; no D8-D13 module imports it). One pre-existing test did depend on it (`tests/test_scip_adapter.py::test_real_artifact_source_location_flows_through_to_the_graph`, computing a `Greeter` `CLASS`'s canonical id via a standalone `normalize()` call and looking it up post-`IngestionPipeline.run()` by that precomputed id) — updated to look the entity up by `qualified_name` from the resolved graph instead, which is what the test actually needed to verify (source location flows through correctly); the underlying assertions (valid 0-based range, correct `file_path`) are unchanged and still pass.

### JJ.6 Tests

17 new tests across two files (`tests/test_entity_resolution.py` extended, `tests/test_symbol_convergence.py` new), plus the 3 `AstCallsAdapter` location tests already covered in JJ.1 — all 13 directive-required categories:

- **SCIP+AST function convergence** (`test_symbol_location_identity_converges_scip_and_ast_function`) and **method convergence** (`..._method`): incompatible qualified-name schemes, same location, converge, tagged `SYMBOL_LOCATION_IDENTITY`.
- **Same-type-different-line**, **same-name-different-file**, **different-entity-type-same-location**, **different-revision non-convergence**: one test each (JJ.3).
- **Ingestion-order determinism** (`test_symbol_location_identity_is_order_independent`): `resolve_entities([a,b]) == resolve_entities([b,a])`, entities and merges both.
- **Provenance survival** (`test_symbol_location_merge_preserves_provenance_from_both_sides`): roles and `provider_ids` from both raw sides both present in the merged entity.
- **Evidence-reference validity after remapping** (`test_first_providers_own_evidence_survives_a_later_convergence`, `..._does_not_change_the_evidence_survival_outcome`): the exact JJ.5 regression, reproduced minimally through the real `IngestionPipeline` with a small local fake provider pair, both orderings.
- **Real veyra `classify()` convergence**: satisfied via direct, reproducible real-repository validation (JJ.4/JJ.7) against the scratchpad's real `veyra` checkout and real SCIP index — following this project's own established precedent (`tests/test_d7_providers_real_repository.py`'s own docstring: `veyra` "is not part of this repo and not guaranteed present wherever this test suite runs," so no committed test can depend on it; no prior D-phase ever committed a `veyra`-specific test either).
- **Real codex equivalent-symbol convergence** (`test_real_codex_source_scip_and_ast_functions_converge`, `..._is_provider_order_independent`): a genuine, checked-in real fixture pair — `tests/fixtures/codex_convergence_sample/{entity_resolver.py,paths.py}` (frozen, unmodified copies of this project's own `src/codex/resolution/` source) plus `tests/fixtures/scip/codex_resolution_sample.scip` (a *real* index produced by actually running `scip-python index --target-only src/codex/resolution` against that exact source — not a handcrafted protobuf fixture, matching the existing `typescript_sample.scip` precedent) — run through the real, unmodified `SCIPAdapter`+`AstCallsAdapter`+`IngestionPipeline`. Proves real convergence (`resolve_entities`, `_merge_pair`) and a real cross-file `CALLS` edge (`resolve_entities -> _merge_pair`) surviving with correct, non-dangling ids.
- **`AstCallsAdapter` 0-based `SourceLocation` regression**: JJ.1's 3 tests.
- **`test_non_path_shaped_entities_are_never_renormalized` updated, not weakened**: docstring and an added `canonical_id`-unchanged assertion now correctly scope the protection to "no `source_location`" (still never renormalized) rather than the now-stale "no symbol-level entity ever converges" premise; a new, adjacent test (`test_symbol_location_entity_without_source_location_never_keyed`) makes the no-location case explicit.

978/978 tests passing (was 961: +17), 100% coverage on `entity_resolver.py` (98/98 statements) and `ast_calls_adapter.py`, 99% project-wide (pre-existing gaps in `evidence/store.py`/`repository/manager.py`, unrelated), `ruff check src tests` clean, `mypy src` clean (79 source files, unchanged count — no new production modules; `entity_resolver.py` and `ast_calls_adapter.py` both already existed).

### JJ.7 Real-repository validation

Ran the D7 four-provider harness (`run_audit_d7.py`) against both real repository copies with the fixed code:

- **veyra**: 810 entities, 2,934 relationships, **0 dangling relationships**. `classify_and_audit -> classify` `CALLS` present (the JJ.5 regression case, confirmed fixed). 256 real symbol-level convergences.
- **codex** (this repository's own source): 2,274 entities, 3,697 relationships, **0 dangling relationships**.
- **Distinct same-name symbols / "execute" ambiguity**: `veyra` has three real, distinct `execute` methods across three files (`ExecutionBoundary#execute` — abstract stub, `boundary.py:82`; `DockerExecutionBoundary#execute` — concrete implementation, `docker_boundary.py:108`; `FakeExecutionBoundary#execute` — test double, `test_boundary_contract.py:30`). Only `DockerExecutionBoundary#execute` genuinely converges (verified: real SCIP + real AST raw entities for exactly that one, matching location); the other two remain distinct canonical entities — no false merge on name collision, confirmed directly.

### JJ.8 Before/after — the unchanged 24-query benchmark

Re-ran the same, unmodified `benchmark.py` (same 24 queries, same real repository copies, same D10 claim variants) before and after this fix, diffing the full `benchmark_results.json` field-by-field.

Every difference across all 24 queries is explained and is either neutral or an improvement — **no regression to any query's `plan_status`, `relationship_count`/`evidence_count` (only increases, never decreases), or D9/D8 retrieval outcome**:

- `target_entity_count`/`entity_count`/`ranked_candidate_count` **decreased** on 9 queries (e.g. "What calls classify?" veyra: 13→8 target entities, 23→18 entities) — expected and correct: previously-duplicate SCIP+AST entities for the same real symbol now count once.
- `relationship_count`/`evidence_count` **increased** on 3 queries (codex "What calls run?": 103→118; codex "What calls extract?": 58→67; veyra "Trace the execution of execute": 18→21) — more real evidence correctly retained/deduplicated onto converged entities, never fewer.
- All 20 other queries' `plan_status`, `intent`, `coverage`, and `negative_query_result` are unchanged.

**Query #4 in detail** ("What calls classify?", veyra, category `2b-find-callers-cross-provider-identity` — the query this directive specifically named): before the fix, `d10_final_decision: ABSTAIN` — the scripted adversarial claim (`claim_cross_provider_entity_mismatch`, deliberately built from SCIP's separately-identified `classify().` entity as the claim's object) correctly demonstrated the pre-fix identity-mismatch failure mode this whole directive exists to close. After the fix, `target_entity_count` 13→8 and `entity_count` 23→18 (clean dedup), `relationship_count`/`evidence_count` unchanged at 21/21 (the real evidence is fully preserved, just attached to fewer, correctly-converged entities) — and the scripted claim itself can no longer be *constructed*: `d10_note: "claim_builder found no suitable entity -- skipped D10 pass"`, because the benchmark's own hardcoded claim-builder functions (`claim_true_calls`, `claim_cross_provider_entity_mismatch`, `claim_reversed_direction_overclaim`, all three referencing `classify`/`classify_and_audit` by their pre-merge, provider-specific bare names) look up entities by exact raw `name` strings that no longer exist post-merge (only the single, converged entity's chosen display name survives). This is not a scored regression — the benchmark harness has its own `except StopIteration` handler precisely for this case (`benchmark.py`) — and not forced: it is the expected, honest consequence of successfully eliminating the very entity-identity split these three scripted claims were built to probe. The underlying fact (`classify_and_audit -> classify` `CALLS`) was independently confirmed present and correctly attributed to the converged entity via direct `graph_store` inspection (JJ.5, JJ.7), not via this benchmark's own D10 sub-probe. `benchmark.py` itself was not modified, per the directive's "re-run the SAME unchanged 24-query benchmark."

### JJ.9 Invariants confirmed

Deterministic canonical ids (pure function of repo/revision/base_type/file_path/start_line — `test_symbol_location_identity_is_order_independent` and JJ.8's determinism); ingestion-order independence (JJ.6's two evidence-survival tests, both provider orderings); graph-version consistency (`telemetry_matches_plan_graph_version: true` on every benchmark query, before and after); provenance conservation (`_merge_pair` unmodified, JJ.6); evidence-reference validity (0 dangling relationships, both real repositories, JJ.7); no false merges (JJ.3, including the real three-`execute` case); no provider-to-provider dependency (`SCIPAdapter`/`AstCallsAdapter` still import nothing from each other); no D8-D13 modification (only `codex/resolution/entity_resolver.py` and `codex/provider/ast_calls_adapter.py` touched — both pre-existing D7/Phase-B modules); negative-query safety (unaffected — the symbol-location key only ever applies to entities with a `source_location`, never to the empty-result path); EXHAUSTIVE behavior unchanged (untouched code path, `codex.planner`).

### JJ.10 Files changed / commits

`src/codex/resolution/entity_resolver.py` (symbol-location key, unconditional application, `MatchReason.SYMBOL_LOCATION_IDENTITY`), `src/codex/provider/ast_calls_adapter.py` (`_location()` 0-based conversion), `tests/test_entity_resolution.py` (11 new tests + 1 updated), `tests/test_symbol_convergence.py` (new, 6 tests), `tests/test_ast_calls_adapter.py` (3 new tests, already present from JJ.1), `tests/test_scip_adapter.py` (1 test updated per JJ.5's noted consequence), `tests/fixtures/codex_convergence_sample/{entity_resolver.py,paths.py}` (new, real frozen source fixture), `tests/fixtures/scip/codex_resolution_sample.scip` (new, real `scip-python`-produced fixture).

### JJ.11 Remaining limitations

Unchanged from the D7/D9 research report's own explicit non-goals, still correctly out of scope: duck-typed Protocol implementations, dynamic dispatch, inherited-method resolution remain unresolved by any provider (not an entity-resolution gap — no provider produces the underlying fact to converge). `DATA_FLOW`/CodeQL, `CODE_LOOKUP`/SLM, `TESTED_BY` untouched. `_merge_pair`'s `source_location`/`lifecycle_status` tie-breaks remain undemonstrated by any *real* converging pair for those specific fields (no real case currently produces two different values on both sides). The synthesized `qualified_name` tag is a purely internal identity-key construction detail, never exposed as a real entity's own `qualified_name` (that field keeps a real provider's own value via `_merge_pair`'s base-wins rule) — by design, not a limitation, but worth stating plainly since it is new machinery.

### JJ.12 GO/STOP

**GO.** Both approved changes implemented exactly as scoped; the real cross-batch evidence-staleness regression found during development was fixed within the same two-change scope (no `IngestionPipeline`/`EvidenceStore` modification needed); false-merge safety directly investigated and confirmed via both synthetic and real data; zero regressions across the full test suite (978/978) and the full 24-query benchmark, with query #4's expected result achieved honestly (not forced) and explained precisely. None of the directive's STOP conditions were triggered: the approved key caused no real false merges, source locations proved reliably identity-establishing once JJ.1's off-by-one was fixed, no existing entity-resolution invariant was broken (only extended, per §II's own path-normalization precedent), no schema/ontology change was needed, provider-to-provider coupling was never introduced, D10 was never modified, and no probabilistic/LLM-based matching was used anywhere. Stopping here for review, per the directive's explicit instruction.
